# Build comprehensive AI-powered internship application system

## You

Let's do a very deep dive into our internship workflow. We aim to build the entire system in this session - research on the company and employees, contacts(HR), internship hunts, record of the process, applying, applied, next steps, interviews, pitches, cover letters, resume modifications, networking (linkedin & handshake), linkedin maxing (posts from my portfolio blog - still developing). That's the almost ready system that I aim to acheive. There should be a well described workflow that starts from here: `10_Areas/Career/Internships` moves to `20_Progress/Internship` for further details on the internship. All the tracking, research, internship listings, networking, contacts and any internships related information should be here: `10_Areas/Career/Internships`. Rest everything that is ongoing - cover letter and resume modifications, posts, applying right now, interview processes per internship and anything that's currently to be done will be here: `20_Progress/Internship`. Now, our first task is going write many prompts to develop what we aim to do here in detail based on all the information on the web about ai internship application help. I want a constant research agent to be running, hunting for internships(contacts, resources, information) - through hermes(maybe), need to figure out how to exactly do this. We then find internships we are applying for and list them here: `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List`. We have a programs folder(`10_Areas/Career/Internships/Programs`) which is going to hold information regarding the company in detail, interlink it to the job application in lists (where all the research lands for the company related to the position). Contacts folder(`10_Areas/Career/Internships/Contacts`) justifies itself here - all the contacts we have found based on the internship, how to reach out to them, how are they related to the intern position, meet them regarding the postings. The note should be heavily interlinked to all the related internship posts, research, etc. Note down the draft email/message we will write to each recruiter based on this note: `10_Areas/Career/Internships/Contacts/Mimic` - will soon have drafts that need to be mimicked across all networking notes. `10_Areas/Career/Internships/Cheats` - cheats to get internships quicker like what we are doing right now. We note down methods, workflows and systems in this folder for source of truths across the internship applying process. This is something that's going to keep on growing and becoming better as we use it more. But before we even spend time to improve it, we built in a manner that we do not think about making it better till we run into a few blockers. This should be perfectly laid out. Now, diving into the `20_Progress/Internship` - we currently have the old 2026 notes inside this folder which can be useful to us, `20_Progress/Internship/Applying` - will be a folder where we note down what posts we will currently be applying for - weekly based. `20_Progress/Internship/Interviews` - stands for itself, all the interviews that will take place will be noten down in detail over here. We will come to this folder when the time comes. Firstly, we focus on the above folders mentioned. Everything that is mentioned to you here is just based on what I have created in obsidian (just folders no good notes). There are gaps in the workflow that i am aiming to acheive, we need to close those gaps before we step into anything over here. First, let's do our research using firecrawl on this workflow that i have described. Search the net for internship/job applying using ai - building systems. After doing your research ask me multiple questions in this session so that we lay out both of the folders mentioned in detail before writing anything. Let's start from scratch, see our end goal clearly and only then build. If you are still unsure about anything, ask me questions. Do not assume anything, read through everything said here in detail. DO not make mistakes, let's do this very accurately and build the system correctly.

Research done. Before I design anything, I need decisions on the parts of your description that are genuinely ambiguous or that conflict with how the vault's Areas/Progress split is supposed to work. Here's the first round — a second round will follow once these land.

Findings on the automation question: **Hermes Agent** (Nous Research) is a real self-hosted autonomous-agent framework with persistent memory and Slack/Telegram integration, but it needs an always-on server — real ops overhead for one task. **Clay** is enrichment (fills in data on a list you already have), not discovery (it won't find new postings on its own) — its free tier is also too thin for continuous polling. The better fit: **Firecrawl's own web-scale monitor** (already available in this vault) can watch the open web for new postings matching a query and post straight to a Slack webhook — free, no new platform, and Claude then reads that Slack channel to structure findings into the vault. That's a real two-step pipeline matching what you described, without burning Claude usage on the polling itself.

## Proposed Architecture

### `10_Areas/Career/Internships/` — reference layer (durable, slow-changing)

```
10_Areas/Career/Internships/
├── Internships Hub.md          NEW — canonical domain hub (the vault has none yet; this becomes the entry point)
├── README.md                   REWRITE — short "how this system works", replaces the stale 2027-cycle setup guide
├── List/
│   └── YYYY-MM Found.md        NEW pattern — one running capture log per month, table of every posting found (company, role, link, date found, quick read, promoted?). Firehose tier — this is where a research feed (Slack/Firecrawl, later) lands.
├── Programs/
│   └── <company-program>.md    EXISTING pattern, TRIMMED — static research only (comp, eligibility, deadlines, traps, prep). Status/dates removed — those move to Applying.
├── Contacts/
│   ├── Mimic.md                FIX — currently has the wrong "concept" template applied; rebuild as a template library of reusable message drafts by scenario
│   └── <person>.md             NEW pattern — facts + inline "Current Draft" section, built from a Mimic template
├── Cheats/
│   └── <method>.md             EXISTING pattern (LinkedIn Premium.md already here) — keep growing ad hoc, standardize frontmatter only
├── Tracker/
│   └── Internship - Dashboard.md   REWRITE queries — static facts pulled from Programs, live status pulled from 20_Progress/Applying (two clearly separated tables, not a magic join — plain Dataview can't join across folders cleanly)
└── Links & Interlinks.md       keep as-is
```

**Retiring:** `Tracker/Tracker.md` (empty Kanban board) — superseded by the Dataview dashboard. Flag if you actually want a visual Kanban kept alongside it.

### `20_Progress/Internship/` — execution layer (active work only)

```
20_Progress/Internship/
├── Applying/
│   ├── _This Week.md           NEW — replaces the empty `AI Applying.md` stub; weekly-curated queue, links out to active program notes
│   └── <company-program>.md    NEW pattern — live status (funnel stage, next action, key dates), links back to the Programs research note, Resumes tailored copy, Contacts, Interview note
├── Interviews/
│   ├── ABB Interview Prep.md   keep
│   ├── Interview Questions.md  keep (generic bank)
│   └── <company>.md            new ones as interviews happen
├── Resumes/
│   ├── Main Resume.pdf         already there
│   ├── Main Resume.md          NEW — editable STAR bullet-bank, the actual tailoring source (PDF isn't editable/taggable)
│   └── Tailored/<company>.md   NEW — per-application tailored resume + cover letter, linked from the Applying note
├── Posts/
│   └── <post-draft>.md         NEW — LinkedIn posts sourced from your portfolio blog, scoped to internship-search content per your answer
├── Career Fair '25 & '26.md    keep as historical mining input, not restructured now
└── Companies giving OPT & CPT.md   keep as historical mining input, not restructured now
```

### Status/funnel stages (single source of truth: the Applying note)
`Researching → Applied → OA/Assessment → Phone Screen → Onsite → Offer / Rejected / Withdrawn` — matches the real-world funnel from the research (Careery's model), not the looser Not Started/Applied/Interview/Offer set currently on Programs.

### Interlinking model
`List` entry → (promoted) → `Programs` note (research) + `Applying` note (live) created together, cross-linked both ways → `Applying` note links `Contacts`, `Resumes/Tailored/*`, `Interviews/*` → everything backlinks to `Internships Hub.md`.

### New templates (in `30_Order/Templates/Career/`, plus one workflow doc)
List Entry, Program (trimmed), Contact, Applying, Interview Prep — and `30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline.md` documenting the full List → Programs+Applying → Contacts → Resume → Interview → Outcome flow, matching the existing Workflows pattern.

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass
Firecrawl-monitor → Slack automation (build folders first, wire in later per your answer), broader LinkedIn personal-brand system (scoped to Internships only), migrating the legacy Career Fair / OPT&CPT notes into the new structure (left as mining material).

# Internship Pipeline
Move a posting from "found on the web" to "offer or rejection recorded," without losing the research or the relationship along the way.
**Use when:** finding, pursuing, or tracking any internship (or later, full-time role — this pipeline is role-type-agnostic by design).
**Splits across:** `10_Areas/Career/Internships/` (reference — research, contacts, methods, stays true regardless of where any single application stands) and `20_Progress/Internship/` (execution — live status, drafts, prep, everything that changes week to week).
## Why It's Split This Way
Company research (comp, eligibility, deadlines, traps) doesn't change once you've written it down — that's Areas. Application status changes every time you hear back — that's Progress. Putting both on one note (the old pattern) meant the "durable" research file was getting rewritten every week just to update a status field. Splitting them means a Program note stays `status: tree`-stable while its Applying note carries all the churn.
## The Pipeline
```
find           List/YYYY-MM Found.md — every posting found this month, one row each
   ↓ decide to seriously pursue it
commit         Programs/<name>.md (static research) + Applying/<name>.md (live status) — created together, cross-linked
   ↓ need a way in beyond the portal
reach out      Contacts/<person>.md — facts + the live unsent draft, built from Contacts/Mimic.md templates
   ↓ need a resume that survives the JD, not just the ATS
tailor         Resumes/Main Resume.md (bullet bank) → Resumes/Tailored/<company>.md (per-application cut)
   ↓ you get a screen or an interview
prep           Interviews/<company>.md — linked from the Applying note's interview_note field
   ↓ outcome lands
close          Applying note status → Offer / Rejected / Withdrawn. Program note stays as reference for next cycle or a referral.
across all of it:  Tracker/Internship - Dashboard.md (detailed, Dataview) + Tracker/Tracker.md (kanban, at-a-glance) · Cheats/ grows whenever something works
```
## Step 1 — Find (List)
Every posting you come across — from a career fair conversation, a GitHub internship-list repo, LinkedIn, Handshake, or (later) an automated feed — gets one row in this month's `List/YYYY-MM Found.md`. Use [[List Monthly Log Template]]. Keep it to a link, the requirements, and one line on what the role actually is. This is not a commitment. Most rows never get promoted, and that's fine — the point is not losing a posting you'd otherwise forget by next week.
## Step 2 — Commit (Programs + Applying)
When a posting is worth the hours to prepare for, promote it: create a Programs note ([[Program Template]]) and an Applying note ([[Applying Template]]) in the same sitting, and link them both ways (`applying_note` on the Program, `program` on the Applying note). The List row's `Promoted` column points at the new Program note.
- **Programs/** holds what won't change: comp, eligibility, deadlines, traps, prep checklist.
- **Applying/** holds what will: funnel stage, next action, dates, links to the resume version and contacts actually used for this specific application.
> [!WARNING]
> If you find yourself editing a Programs note to change a status word, stop — that field belongs on the Applying note. Programs notes should only change when a fact about the program itself changes (deadline moved, comp updated).
## Step 3 — Reach Out (Contacts)
A Contact note ([[Contact Template]]) holds durable facts about a person and, inline, the current unsent draft message to them. `Contacts/Mimic.md` is the template library — cold DM, recruiter follow-up, referral ask, thank-you note — pull a template into the Current Draft section and edit it until it sounds like you, not the template. Log every real exchange in Conversation Log so a later Claude Code session (or you, six weeks from now) knows what's already been said.
This is drafting help only. No message sends itself — every draft here is reviewed and sent by hand.
## Step 4 — Tailor (Resumes)
`Resumes/Main Resume.md` is the editable bullet bank — the actual source of truth, since the PDF isn't taggable or diffable. Per the research behind this system: response rate moves most when you adjust the **top third** of the resume per role, not when you rewrite the whole thing. For each Applying note, create `Resumes/Tailored/<company>.md`: pull the 3-5 bullets that best match this specific JD's keywords, in order, and link it back from the Applying note's `resume_version` field.
## Step 5 — Prep (Interviews)
Once a program moves to Phone Screen or Onsite, create `Interviews/<company>.md` and link it from the Applying note's `interview_note` field. `Interview Questions.md` in the same folder is the generic bank — pull from it, don't duplicate it.
## Step 6 — Track (two views, one truth)
- **`Tracker/Internship - Dashboard.md`** — the detailed view. Dataview queries pull static facts from Programs and live status from Applying, shown as separate tables (plain Dataview can't cleanly join two folders into one row, so don't try to fake a merge).
- **`Tracker/Tracker.md`** — the kanban view. A fast visual glance across Interesting → To Apply → Applying Today → Applied, cards linked to the actual Applying notes. Update it by hand when a card's stage changes; it is not meant to be exhaustive, the Dashboard is.
## Step 7 — Close
When an outcome lands, update the Applying note's `status` to `Offer`, `Rejected`, or `Withdrawn` and add the final Log entry. Leave the Program note alone — it's still valid reference if you (or a friend) reapply next cycle.
## Cadence
- **As you find things:** add a row to this month's List log. Thirty seconds, no exceptions.
- **Weekly (the Friday ritual):** work through `Applying/_This Week.md` — which programs are actually in motion, what's due, what needs a follow-up.
- **Per application:** the full Step 2 through Step 5 sequence happens once, when you commit.
## Cheats
`Cheats/` is where anything that actually worked gets written down — a tactic, a shortcut, a system, using [[Cheat Template]]. It grows only from real results, not speculation, and it is explicitly not being redesigned preemptively: the instruction behind this folder is to use it as-is until it produces a real blocker, then fix that blocker, not to polish it now.
## Deferred: Automated Discovery
The long-term goal is a 24/7 feed that watches the web for new postings and drops them into a Slack channel, which Claude then reads and turns into List rows automatically — using Firecrawl's monitor feature rather than a third-party platform, since Clay is enrichment (not discovery) and Hermes Agent needs a self-hosted always-on server for a single-purpose task. This is explicitly not built yet. The pipeline above works by hand first; automation gets wired into Step 1 once the manual version is proven.
## Frontmatter Quick Reference
| Note | Type | Lives in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| List monthly log | `input` | `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/` |
| Program | (no `type` — reference data) | `10_Areas/Career/Internships/Programs/` |
| Contact | `contact` | `10_Areas/Career/Internships/Contacts/` |
| Cheat | `evergreen` | `10_Areas/Career/Internships/Cheats/` |
| Applying | `project` | `20_Progress/Internship/Applying/` |
| Interview prep | `project` or `input` | `20_Progress/Internship/Interviews/` |
| Tailored resume | (no `type` — working document) | `20_Progress/Internship/Resumes/Tailored/` |
| LinkedIn post | `output` | `20_Progress/Internship/Posts/` |
## Done When
- Every posting you'd regret forgetting has a List row.
- Every program you're actually pursuing has both a Programs note and an Applying note, cross-linked.
- No Applying note has gone more than a week without a Log entry while it's active.
- The Dashboard and the Kanban agree on what's currently in motion.
- The session log records any structural change to this pipeline.

# Internships Hub
==This folder is reference: research on companies, people, and methods that stays true regardless of where any single application currently stands.== Live status, drafts, and prep live in `20_Progress/Internship/` instead — see [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]] for the full flow between the two.
## What's Here
- **`List/`** — monthly capture logs of every posting found. Firehose tier, no commitment implied.
- **`Programs/`** — one deep-dive note per program you've committed to pursuing. Static research only: comp, eligibility, deadlines, traps.
- **`Contacts/`** — one note per person, durable facts plus the current live draft to them. `Mimic.md` holds the reusable message templates.
- **`Cheats/`** — methods and tactics that actually worked, added as they're proven, not designed in advance.
- **`Tracker/`** — the two dashboard views: `Internship - Dashboard.md` (detailed, Dataview) and `Tracker.md` (kanban, at-a-glance).
## Where The Live Work Is
`20_Progress/Internship/` holds everything that changes week to week: `Applying/` (live status per program), `Interviews/` (prep per company), `Resumes/` (the bullet bank and tailored cuts), `Posts/` (LinkedIn drafts).
## Why The Split
A Program note that also carries a status field gets rewritten every time you hear back — that defeats the point of Areas being durable. [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]] explains the mechanism in full; this note is the entry point, not the explanation.
## Scope
Built internship-first but role-type-agnostic — the same List/Programs/Contacts/Cheats structure is meant to carry a full-time job search later without renaming or restructuring.

# Mimic
==Reusable message drafts by scenario. Every Contact note's Current Draft section starts here, then gets edited until it sounds like you, not the template.== This is a template library, not a contact — it earns new entries only from drafts that actually got a response, not speculation.
## Cold LinkedIn DM
Use when you have no prior connection and want a low-pressure opener, not an ask.
```
Hi [Name],
I came across the internship role at [Company] and liked how your team works on [specific thing — early-career hiring, the actual product, a recent post]. Would love to connect and learn more.
```
*Why it works:* no ask in the first message. It's a connection request with context, not a cold pitch — lowers the bar for a reply.
## Recruiter Follow-Up
Use after applying, roughly one to two weeks in, or when a recruiter has gone quiet.
```
Hi [Name],
I applied to [Role] at [Company] on [date] and wanted to check in — is there any update on timeline, or additional info I can provide? I'm still very interested in the team's work on [specific thing].
Thanks for your time,
[Your name]
```
*Why it works:* references a real date and a specific detail, so it doesn't read as a template. Respond to any recruiter reply within 24 hours — interview slots fill in batches.
## Referral Ask
Use with someone warm (alumni, past intern, mutual connection) once you already have a relationship, not as a first message.
```
Hi [Name],
I just applied to [Role] at [Company] and remembered you work there. Would you be open to referring me, or pointing me to whoever handles that? Happy to send my resume and a quick summary of why I'm a fit.
```
*Why it works:* the ask is specific and low-effort for them (a referral click, not a favor). Treat a referral as one input, not a guarantee — it does not reliably beat a strong direct application.
## Thank-You After Interview
Send within 24 hours, referencing something specific from the conversation, not a generic thanks.
```
Hi [Name],
Thank you for taking the time to talk through [specific thing discussed — a project, a team challenge]. It confirmed why I'm excited about [Company] specifically, especially [specific detail]. Looking forward to hearing next steps.
```
*Why it works:* the specificity is the entire point — a generic thank-you is indistinguishable from a template and reads as one.
## LinkedIn Premium Request
See [[LinkedIn Premium]] in Cheats for the full template and context — it's a request-for-access script, not an outreach message, so it lives there instead of being duplicated here.

```

# 📊 Internship Tracker Dashboard
Two kinds of query below, and they don't mix: **research** tables pull static facts from `Programs/` (comp, deadlines, eligibility — these don't change once written). **Pipeline** tables pull live status from `20_Progress/Internship/Applying/` (funnel stage, dates — these change every time you hear back). See [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]] for why they're split. The kanban glance view is [[Tracker]].
## ⏰ Research — Upcoming Deadlines (Next 30 Days)
```dataview
TABLE
  company,
  opens_date,
  deadline_real as "Real Deadline",
  pay_per_week as "Pay/Week",
  eligible_classes as "Eligible"
FROM "10_Areas/Career/Internships/Programs"
WHERE deadline_real <= date(today) + dur(30 days)
SORT deadline_real ASC
```
> [!WARNING]
> Real deadlines run ahead of posted deadlines on most programs — apply in the first two weeks a portal opens, don't wait for the posted date.
## 🔄 Pipeline — Live Status
```dataview
TABLE WITHOUT ID
  file.link as "Application",
  company as "Company",
  status as "Stage",
  date_applied as "Applied",
  next_deadline as "Next Deadline"
FROM "20_Progress/Internship/Applying"
WHERE status != "Rejected" AND status != "Withdrawn"
SORT next_deadline ASC
```
### Offer / Rejected / Withdrawn
```dataview
TABLE WITHOUT ID
  file.link as "Application",
  status as "Result",
  date_response as "Date"
FROM "20_Progress/Internship/Applying"
WHERE status IN ("Offer", "Rejected", "Withdrawn")
SORT date_response DESC
```
### Funnel Counts
```dataview
TABLE WITHOUT ID
  rows.status as "Status",
  length(rows) as "Count"
FROM "20_Progress/Internship/Applying"
GROUP BY status
```
## 💰 Research — Sorted By Compensation
```dataview
TABLE
  company as "Company",
  program_type as "Type",
  pay_per_week as "Pay/Week",
  deadline_real as "Deadline"
FROM "10_Areas/Career/Internships/Programs"
WHERE pay_per_week
SORT pay_per_week DESC
```
## 🌊 Research — By Wave
```dataview
TABLE
  company,
  eligible_classes,
  opens_date,
  deadline_real,
  pay_per_week
FROM "10_Areas/Career/Internships/Programs"
WHERE wave
SORT wave ASC, deadline_real ASC
```
## ✅ Research — Total Programs Tracked
```dataview
LIST
FROM "10_Areas/Career/Internships/Programs"
WHERE name
```
## 🎯 This Week
Work this from `20_Progress/Internship/Applying/_This Week.md`, not from here — this dashboard is for scanning, that note is for doing.
## 📚 External Resources
See [[Links & Interlinks]] for job boards, career fair links, and mock-interview resources.

# Internship System — Build Log
==This is the session record for designing and building the internship application system spanning `10_Areas/Career/Internships/` and `20_Progress/Internship/` on 2026-07-16.== The workflow doc ([[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]]) is the lean operating procedure; this note is the full design rationale — why each decision landed where it did, what the research actually said, and what was built versus deferred.
## Goal
Build the full internship pipeline: company/employee research, HR/recruiter contacts, listings, application tracking, next steps, interview prep, pitches, cover letters, resume tailoring, LinkedIn/Handshake networking, and LinkedIn content from the portfolio blog. Split cleanly across `10_Areas` (reference, durable) and `20_Progress` (execution, live) rather than the single-tier setup that existed before this session.
## Starting State
Before this session: `10_Areas/Career/Internships/` had `Programs/` (one real note, HRT, with status fields baked into static research), `Tracker/` (a Dataview dashboard querying Programs' status field directly, plus an empty 4-lane Kanban), `Contacts/Mimic.md` (had the wrong "concept" template auto-applied — One-Line Answer/Mechanism/Flashcards structure meant for course concepts, not a message-template library), `Cheats/LinkedIn Premium.md` (one real cheat), and an empty `List/` folder. `20_Progress/Internship/` had legacy 2026-cycle notes (Career Fair, Companies OPT&CPT), an empty `AI Applying.md` stub, and two real interview-prep notes.
## Research Findings
### Tracking Funnel Structure
Real data (careery.pro): **Applied → Response (10-15%) → Phone Screen (50% of responses) → Final Interview (50% of screens) → Offer (30-50% of finals)**. About 100 cold applications produces 1-2 offers. A response rate under 10% signals a resume/targeting problem; responses without offers signal an interview-prep problem — this diagnostic split is why the pipeline needs live status broken out by stage, not a single "Applied" bucket. CS/tech students typically need 100-300+ applications; other fields need far fewer because networking substitutes for volume.
### Personal CRM Patterns
Tools reviewed (Dex, Clay, Orvo, folk) converge on the same fields: last-contact date, relationship tags, a structured note per real conversation, and reconnection nudges. The named failure mode, repeated across sources: spreadsheet/Notion-as-CRM has no reminders, so people "stop updating after week two." This is why the Contact note ([[Contact Template]]) keeps a Conversation Log inline rather than relying on a separate tracker nobody revisits.
### AI-Assisted Workflow Patterns
The cleanest framing found (careery.pro): automate the mechanical parts (form-fill, tracking, listing alerts — cuts 15-20 minutes per application to 2-5) and keep human judgment for role fit, resume customization, networking, and interview prep. Don't automate the differentiators. This directly shaped the decision to keep all outreach drafting as human-reviewed, never auto-sent (see [[Mimic]]).
### Resume/Cover Letter Tailoring
An empirical account (Kaizen Conroy) improved response rate from under 5% to 30%+ by adjusting the **top third** of the resume per role rather than rewriting the whole document, keeping 2-3 base variants, and putting tech stack inline next to each project rather than in a separate skills list. This is the direct source for [[Main Resume.md]]'s tagged-bullet-bank design and the Tailoring Checklist on it.
### LinkedIn "Maxing"
Gap in the research: none of the search results surfaced concrete blog-to-post tactics or posting cadence. The only adjacent finding was LinkedIn job-change alerts used as a CRM reconnection trigger, not a growth tactic. This is why `Posts/` was built as a thin scaffold rather than a designed system — there's nothing solid to design against yet.
### Cold Outreach and Anti-Patterns
Concrete cadence signals: respond to recruiter replies within 24 hours (interview slots fill in batches), apply within 48 hours of a posting (odds drop sharply after), send interview thank-yous within 24 hours referencing something specific. Anti-patterns: volume without diagnosis (more applications doesn't fix a broken funnel — find which stage is actually broken first), referrals aren't automatically better (one account: 3 referred applications, zero interviews), ghost jobs and stale postings waste effort, and over-automated outreach on LinkedIn carries real platform risk (documented account restrictions from automated activity).
### Research Automation Platforms (Hermes / Clay / Firecrawl)
**Hermes Agent** (Nous Research) is a real, MIT-licensed, self-hosted autonomous agent with persistent cross-session memory and native Slack/Telegram/Discord integrations — a genuine conceptual fit, but it needs an always-on server, which is real ops overhead for one task. **Clay** is *enrichment*, not *discovery* — it fills in data on a list you already have; it does not sit and watch the web for new postings appearing, and its free tier (100 credits/month) is too thin for continuous polling anyway. **Firecrawl's own web-scale monitor** (already available in this vault as a skill) can watch search queries across the whole web for new matching content and push to a webhook, with a built-in AI judge filtering noise — this is the strongest free fit since it needs no new platform.
> [!NOTE]
> Recommended future architecture: Firecrawl monitor (Step 1, discovery) → Slack webhook → Claude reads the channel periodically and structures confirmed leads into `List/` (Step 2). Clay's free tier is reserved for occasional on-demand contact enrichment of companies that already cleared discovery, not continuous polling. This is documented as deferred — see Decisions below.
## Decisions Made
### Round 1
- **Status ownership:** split. `Programs/` (10_Areas) holds only static research; a paired note per program in `Applying/` (20_Progress) holds live status. Reason given: matches the vault's own Areas/Progress split — a "durable" file that gets rewritten every week to update a status field defeats the purpose of Areas.
- **List vs Programs:** List is the firehose (every posting found, low effort), Programs is committed (only created once you decide to seriously pursue something). A List row is promoted, not automatically mirrored.
- **Contact drafts:** live inline in the Contact note itself, under a Current Draft section, built from `Mimic.md` templates. One file per person, not two.
- **"Hermes":** clarified as Nous Research's Hermes Agent, evaluated above — not adopted this session, folded into the deferred-automation note in the workflow doc.
### Round 2
- **Automation timing:** build the folder structure first; wire the Firecrawl-monitor-to-Slack pipeline in later, once the manual system is proven.
- **Resume system:** everything lives in `20_Progress/Internship/Resumes/` — no separate 10_Areas resume hub. `Main Resume.pdf` was already placed by the user before this session; `Main Resume.md` (the editable bullet bank) was built from reading that PDF.
- **LinkedIn scope:** scoped under Internships for now (`20_Progress/Internship/Posts/`), not a broader personal-brand system.
- **System scope:** generic pipeline, internships as current focus — frontmatter uses `role_type` and keeps `program_type`/`wave` optional rather than internship-hardcoded, so the same structure carries a full-time job search later without renaming folders.
### From the follow-up message (List/Tracker/Workflow/Build-Log specifics)
- List folder holds monthly capture logs (`YYYY-MM Found.md`), one table row per posting — link, requirements, one line on what it is. Confirmed as the pattern, not one file per posting.
- `Tracker/Tracker.md` (the 4-lane Kanban) is kept, not retired — it's a second, faster glance view alongside the detailed Dataview dashboard, not redundant with it. Cards link to real Applying notes.
- `30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline.md` covers the full flow end to end, including how it's actually run (cadence, the deferred automation note).
- This note exists specifically to hold more implementation detail and research rationale than the workflow doc carries.

## Note-Pattern Reference
### List Monthly Log
`type: input`, `input_kind: listing`. One file per month. Table columns: Company, Role, Link, Found, Requirements, What It Is, Promoted. A row's Promoted column becomes a wikilink once it graduates to a Program.
### Program
No `type:` field (pure structured reference data). Frontmatter: `name`, `company`, `program_type`, `eligible_classes`, `grad_year`, `role_type` (generic-scope field), `wave`, `opens_date`, `deadline_posted`, `deadline_real`, `pay_per_week`, `pay_currency`, `duration_weeks`, `benefits`, `application_url`, `careers_page`, `list_origin`, `applying_note`, `recruiter_contact`, `tags`. Body: Program Overview, Eligibility, Traps & Gotchas, Prep Checklist, Related Resources. No status field — that lives on the paired Applying note.
### Contact
`type: contact`. Frontmatter: `name`, `role`, `company`, `linkedin_url`, `email`, `how_found`, `relationship` (cold/warm/referral/alumni), `related_programs`, `last_contact_date`, `next`. Body: Facts, Current Draft, Conversation Log, Next Action.
### Applying
`type: project` (matches `20_Progress` convention, uses [[Project Standard]]). Frontmatter: `status` (Researching/Applied/OA/Phone Screen/Onsite/Offer/Rejected/Withdrawn), `program` (link back to the Program note), `company`, `date_applied`, `date_response`, `next_deadline`, `resume_version`, `cover_letter`, `contacts`, `interview_note`, `next`. Body: Goal, Current State, Next Action, Open Questions, Log — the standard Project Standard shape.
### Cheat
`type: evergreen`. No fixed body structure beyond What It Is, Why It Works, How To Use It, Failure Modes, Evidence — grows one note at a time from proven results, deliberately not over-designed.
### Interview Prep
Organic pattern already established by `ABB Interview Prep.md` — no new template forced onto it; new interview notes should follow that existing example (Job Description Analysis, Elevator Pitch, Talking Points, Questions to be Prepared for, Questions to Ask) rather than a generic template.

## You

Now, let's focus on building the researching automation. The notes written so far lay out the system that needs to be built in detail. The tracking system is still not laid out entirely, we make it the easiest for this tracking to take place. I am going to provide you with a lot of information regarding a session with gemini about the research loop - information about company and employees, contact information, networking and ofcourse how exactly is this related to me and my profile. We had a discussion on how exactly will the research take place using web scrappers at a cheap cost or no cost, returned this: ```## 1. How to Defeat CAPTCHAs Fast, Cheaply, and Token-Efficiently

Since AI Computer Use is too expensive, you have two primary, low-cost ways to bypass CAPTCHAs and bot blocks without using any LLM tokens:

- The Session Reuse Method (Zero Cost):  
    Most CAPTCHAs appear when a site detects a new, unauthenticated browser or a login screen. You can bypass this completely by logging into the target sites (like LinkedIn) manually on your computer _once_, exporting your browser’s cookies and session state via a browser extension (like "EditThisCookie"), and loading those cookies directly into your Playwright or Crawl4AI script. The website will see your scraper as an already logged-in, trusted user session and will skip the CAPTCHA entirely.
- API-Based CAPTCHA Solvers (Fractions of a Cent):  
    Instead of using an LLM to solve a CAPTCHA, use programmatic solving APIs like 2Captcha, CapSolver, or Anti-Captcha. When your local scraper encounters a CAPTCHA, it extracts the site key or image, sends it to the API via an HTTP request, receives the text token back in under 10 seconds, and injects it into the page. This costs roughly ₹10 to ₹50 ($0.12 to $0.60) per _one thousand_ solved CAPTCHAs, consuming zero LLM tokens.

---

## 2. Which Method is the Absolute Best for Your Use Case?

The Custom Chromium Sandbox (Stealth Browser) combined with Firecrawl is your best hybrid approach.

```unset
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE WINNER                                │
│          Custom Chromium Sandbox (Playwright) + Firecrawl API          │
├───────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┤
│ WHY IT WINS:                      │ HOW TO DEPLOY IT:                  │
│ • Bypasses Cloudflare easily.     │ • Use Firecrawl for tough public   │
│ • Blends in with regular users.   │   company pages/blogs.             │
│ • Running costs are ultra-low.    │ • Use local Playwright Stealth for │
│ • Completely token-free.          │   session-locked networking tools. │
└───────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Why this combination beats the others:

- Traditional Scraping (Crawl4AI vanilla) gets flagged too fast by security systems like Cloudflare because its basic browser fingerprints scream "bot."
- Computer Use is an expensive gimmick for a 24/7 background worker.
- Custom Chromium with Stealth patches modifies your browser's underlying JavaScript engines, hides automation variables (`navigator.webdriver`), and randomizes user behavior. It behaves precisely like a human browsing the web, but processes data at computer speeds for zero token cost.

---

## 3. Are Web Scrapers Alright for Finding Resources?

Yes, web scrapers are perfect for the discovery/resource phase.

You do not need heavy stealth engineering to find your initial job listings. Open-source tracking lists (like the Pitt CSC repository) or standard aggregate job feeds are highly accessible. They are served as public, relatively static markdown files or basic HTML pages.

Because these resource pages do not hide behind aggressive login gates or high-tier anti-bot walls, Firecrawl or Crawl4AI can scrape them flawlessly in seconds. You only need to scale up your stealth tactics later when your pipeline pivots to pulling internal company engineering data and locating employee contact nodes.

**One architectural objection before we build, not a moral aside — a risk-engineering one:**

The CapSolver + stealth-Playwright layer for bypassing CAPTCHAs to scrape personal contact data (this is clearly aimed at LinkedIn, given "HR/Engineering Lead contacts") is the one component I won't help build. Two concrete reasons a systems architect should also care about:

1. **ToS/legal exposure compounds with the outreach step.** Contact data obtained via anti-bot-evasion + used for unsolicited outreach is a materially worse fact pattern than either piece alone (CFAA-adjacent access theory + CAN-SPAM-adjacent sourcing). This is the actual failure mode that gets automation projects like this shut down or gets accounts banned mid-campaign — not a hypothetical.
2. **It's also just brittle.** CAPTCHA-solving arms races mean your ingestion layer breaks weekly and you're maintaining an adversarial dependency instead of your actual product (the research/outreach quality).

**Substitute that keeps the pipeline 100% intact:** contact discovery via legitimate channels — company `/about`, `/team`, engineering blog author bylines, GitHub org public member emails, conference-speaker bios, and pattern-inference (`first.last@domain.com`) validated against public MX/SMTP-safe checks (no login walls touched). I'll architect a `contact_resolver.py` module against these sources below. If you specifically need LinkedIn data, that's a manual step you do yourself in a browser — Claude drafts the outreach once you paste in the name/title.

Everything else — ingestion, dedup, enrichment, drafting, orchestration — proceeds exactly as scoped.

## 1. Live Sources (verified today)

Skip Firecrawl/Crawl4AI entirely for the primary feed — these repos expose structured data directly, no HTML scraping needed:

| Source | Endpoint | Format |

The Simplify repo's internship data lives in .github/scripts/listings.json, with entries carrying company_name, locations, title, date_posted, terms, active, url, is_visible, source, company_url, date_updated, and a stable id field. That `id` (UUID) is a gift — use it as your primary dedup key before falling back to content-hashing. The file is regenerated by scheduled GitHub Actions roughly every 30 minutes ("Updating listings.json with postings from Simplify"), so polling the raw URL every 15–30 min via ETag/If-Modified-Since is sufficient — no need to git-clone.

vanshb03/Summer2027-Internships is the successor repo for Summer 2027 cycle internships, maintained collaboratively by Vansh and CSCareers, and modeled on the Pitt CSC & Simplify structure — same JSON schema, same integration path. Note the transition timing: tech internships for summer 2027 open July–October 2026, with Amazon and Databricks typically posting first, so both repos are live simultaneously right now — poll both.

## 2. Ingestion + Dedup Blueprint

**Dedup key strategy (two-tier, since upstream `id` isn't always stable across forks/off-season lists):**

```python
# core/identity.py
import hashlib, re

def compute_uid(company: str, title: str, locations: list[str], upstream_id: str | None) -> str:
    """
    Tier 1: upstream UUID if present (Simplify/vansh listings.json) — authoritative.
    Tier 2: normalized content hash — fallback for markdown-only sources.
    """
    if upstream_id:
        return f"src:{upstream_id}"

    norm = lambda s: re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9]', '', s.lower())
    canonical = norm(company) + "|" + norm(title) + "|" + norm(sorted(locations)[0] if locations else "")
    return "hash:" + hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
```

**Vault as source of truth, SQLite as derived index** (avoids O(n) frontmatter walk every poll cycle, but never trusts the cache over the vault):

```python
# core/vault_index.py
import sqlite3, frontmatter, pathlib

VAULT = pathlib.Path("~/Obsidian/Internships").expanduser()
INDEX_DB = VAULT / ".dedupe_index.sqlite3"

def rebuild_index():
    """Full re-scan. Run on worker startup and daily as a drift-correction pass —
    catches manual edits/deletes you made in Obsidian directly."""
    conn = sqlite3.connect(INDEX_DB)
    conn.execute("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS uid_map (uid TEXT PRIMARY KEY, filepath TEXT, status TEXT, last_seen TEXT)")
    conn.execute("DELETE FROM uid_map")
    for md_file in VAULT.rglob("*.md"):
        post = frontmatter.load(md_file)
        uid = post.get("uid")
        if uid:
            conn.execute(
                "INSERT OR REPLACE INTO uid_map VALUES (?,?,?,?)",
                (uid, str(md_file.relative_to(VAULT)), post.get("status", "unknown"), post.get("last_seen", ""))
            )
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()

def exists(uid: str) -> bool:
    conn = sqlite3.connect(INDEX_DB)
    row = conn.execute("SELECT 1 FROM uid_map WHERE uid = ?", (uid,)).fetchone()
    conn.close()
    return row is not None
```

Zero-dedup guarantee comes from: `rebuild_index()` on every worker boot (cheap, <1s for thousands of notes) + incremental `INSERT OR REPLACE` after each new note write. The SQLite file is disposable — delete it and it self-heals from vault frontmatter on next boot. Vault stays canonical.

## 3. Module Map

```
internship_loop/
├── ingestion/
│   ├── sources.py          # poll listings.json (SimplifyJobs, vanshb03) via requests + ETag caching
│   └── normalize.py        # map raw JSON → internal Listing dataclass
├── core/
│   ├── identity.py         # compute_uid()
│   └── vault_index.py      # SQLite dedup cache
├── enrichment/
│   ├── company_research.py # Firecrawl/Crawl4AI on public company sites, eng blogs, GitHub org
│   ├── tech_stack.py       # keyword/NER extraction from JD + blog text
│   └── contact_resolver.py # legitimate contact discovery (see below — replaces the CapSolver ask)
├── drafting/
│   └── gmail_draft.py      # Gmail API create_draft (never send) — via existing Gmail connector
├── vault/
│   ├── templates/dossier.md.j2
│   └── writer.py           # Jinja2 render → vault write, idempotent on uid
└── orchestrator/
    ├── pipeline.py         # state machine: NEW→ENRICHED→CONTACT_FOUND→DRAFTED→ARCHIVED
    └── worker.py            # entrypoint, called by systemd timer
```

**`contact_resolver.py` approach** (no auth walls touched):

```python
# enrichment/contact_resolver.py
import itertools

def candidate_emails(first: str, last: str, domain: str) -> list[str]:
    f, l = first.lower(), last.lower()
    patterns = [f"{f}.{l}", f"{f[0]}{l}", f"{f}{l[0]}", f"{f}"]
    return [f"{p}@{domain}" for p in patterns]

# Name/title sourcing: engineering blog author bylines (public), GitHub org
# member public profile emails, conference speaker pages. No login-walled
# platforms touched. Validate candidates via MX record check (dnspython) +
# optional SMTP RCPT probe (rate-limited, single attempt) — never bulk-verify
# through a third-party API that itself scrapes LinkedIn.
```

## 4. Orchestration — systemd (not cron: gives you logging, restart policy, resource caps for free)

I'd skip "Hermes Agent" — I don't have reliable information on a framework by that name and won't guess at an API surface for you to build against. For a single-host 24/7 loop, systemd timer + a plain Python entrypoint is more robust than adding an orchestration framework dependency; if you outgrow single-host later, that's when APScheduler-in-process or Prefect earns its keep.

```ini
# /etc/systemd/system/internship-loop.service
[Unit]
Description=Internship Intel Loop
After=network-online.target

[Service]
Type=oneshot
ExecStart=/home/anant/venvs/intern-loop/bin/python -m orchestrator.worker
WorkingDirectory=/home/anant/internship_loop
Environment=PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1
MemoryMax=1G
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=30
```

```ini
# /etc/systemd/system/internship-loop.timer
[Unit]
Description=Run internship loop every 20 min

[Timer]
OnBootSec=2min
OnUnitActiveSec=20min
Persistent=true

[Install]
WantedBy=timers.target
```

`systemctl enable --now internship-loop.timer` — survives reboot, logs to `journalctl -u internship-loop`, auto-retries on transient failure (rate limits, network blips) without a runaway process.

## Claude

Let me dig into these repos properly before I weigh in on architecture — delegating the raw research to keep my own context clean for the synthesis.

Went deep on all six repos plus fact-checked the Gemini/Sonnet transcript's claims against the live data. Here's what's actually true, then my proposed architecture, then the questions I need answered before building anything.

## What the repos actually are

| Repo | Verdict |
|---|---|
| **SimplifyJobs/Summer2026-Internships** | Real, live, huge. `listings.json` on the **`dev`** branch (not `main`), 14,946 entries, confirmed ~30-min update cadence from actual commit timestamps. Schema confirmed: `company_name, title, url, locations, terms, category, degrees, id, date_posted, date_updated, source, sponsorship, active, is_visible`. General SWE board — not sophomore-specific, needs filtering. |
| **Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez/underclassmen-opportunities** | Real, live, small (112 entries). `listings.json` on `main`. **Has an explicit `target_year` array** (`["Freshman (1st year)", "Sophomore (2nd year)"]`) — more precisely typed to your eligibility than SimplifyJobs. Event-driven updates (issue-approval pipeline) plus daily/weekly cron badges. |
| **zapplyjobs/underclassmen-internships** | README-only, no JSON feed, manually curated, weekly-ish edits. Would need scraping the README table, not an API call. |
| **LuisaE/opportunities** | Dead. Last commit December 2023. Skip it. |
| **interviewstreet/hiring-agent** | Wrong category entirely — it's an AI resume-*scoring* tool for employers, not a listings source. Not useful here. |
| **yangshun/tech-interview-handbook** | Confirmed as interview-prep content (Grind 75, behavioral guides) — reference material for Cheats/Interviews later, not an automation source. |

One correction to the Gemini/Sonnet transcript: it claimed listings.json is "regenerated by scheduled GitHub Actions." Not quite — there's no cron-triggered workflow in the repo. An external bot pushes directly to `dev`; the in-repo Actions only react to that push (relint, regenerate README). Doesn't change the plan, but the mechanism was wrong.

## The architecture I'd propose

```
Layer 1  Discovery        Poll SimplifyJobs + Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez listings.json every 30-60 min
         (zero LLM cost)  Plain HTTP GET + ETag caching. No scraping engine needed — it's already JSON.

Layer 2  Filter            Match each entry against your profile (sophomore, 2028 grad, SWE/AI/data,
         (zero LLM cost)   Summer 2027, US) using the feeds' own structured fields — pure field
                           matching, no model call. Turns ~15k raw entries into maybe 50-150 relevant ones.

Layer 3  Dedup              Both feeds already carry a stable `id` — use it directly as the dedup key.
         (zero LLM cost)   No hashing gymnastics needed for these two sources.

Layer 4  Land               Filtered + deduped matches get written into the vault as basic dossiers —
         (zero LLM cost)   structured fields only, no research yet.

Layer 5  Enrich             Company research (tech stack, eng blog) via Firecrawl scrape — cheap API
         (Firecrawl only,  credits, zero LLM tokens for the scrape itself. Contact discovery via
         no Claude)        legitimate sources only (see below) — also scriptable, no LLM required.

Layer 6  Judgment           You or Claude Code review the small curated batch, decide what to promote
         (Claude, bounded, to a real Program + Applying pair, draft outreach from Mimic. This is the
         infrequent)       only layer that should ever spend Claude tokens, and it runs on ~10-20
                           items a week, not 15,000 raw entries a day.
```

## Direct answers to what you asked

**"Should Claude handle all of this? Won't it burn tokens?"** No, and it shouldn't. Layers 1-4 are pure structured-data matching against JSON you already have — there's no reasoning task there, so there's no reason to spend a model call on it. Claude only belongs at Layer 6, on a pre-filtered batch, on a schedule you control (weekly, or on-demand).

**"I want other services, almost no cost."** GitHub Actions is the fit: free (unlimited minutes on public repos, 2,000 free minutes/month on private), cron-scheduled natively, and it solves a problem the Gemini/Sonnet transcript didn't account for — **it recommended `systemd`, which doesn't exist on Windows.** Your machine is Windows 11. `systemd` timers are a non-starter here. Options that actually work: GitHub Actions (runs in the cloud, doesn't care if your laptop is on), or Windows Task Scheduler (local, free, but only fires when the machine is awake). GitHub Actions is the more "24/7" of the two.

**The CAPTCHA/LinkedIn question.** I agree with the Sonnet session's call and won't build the cookie-injection/CapSolver/stealth-browser layer against LinkedIn specifically — same two reasons it gave: it's the exact fact pattern (automated access evasion + unsolicited outreach) that gets accounts banned, and it's a maintenance trap (CAPTCHA vendors and site defenses both keep moving). The substitute holds: company `/about` and `/team` pages, GitHub org public members, engineering-blog bylines, conference-speaker bios, and pattern-inferred email (`first.last@domain.com`) validated against MX records — all scriptable, zero login walls touched. If you specifically want a LinkedIn contact, that's still a manual lookup you do yourself; Claude drafts the message once you paste in a name and title.

## One design conflict I need you to resolve

Last session we built `List/YYYY-MM Found.md` as a simple monthly **table** — one row per posting, deliberately lightweight. But you now want "excess detail" per posting (contacts, company research) attached to automated finds. A table row can't hold that. The two options:

1. **Automated finds get their own file per posting** (a lightweight dossier with frontmatter for dedup, holding the enrichment), and the monthly table becomes a Dataview query *generated from* those files instead of hand-typed — so it still looks like one simple list, but the depth lives underneath.
2. **Keep List/ exactly as built** (manual quick-adds only) and give automated discovery a separate new folder entirely, so manual and automated finds never mix.

I lean toward option 1 — it keeps one mental model instead of two — but this changes the pattern we just built, so I want your call before touching it.

Before I go further on the GitHub Actions plan — I need to flag something I just found.

**Your `Jarvis` vault repo is public on GitHub, has an auto-commit-and-push running roughly every 2 hours, and the branch is already fully pushed to `origin`.** Concretely:
- `20_Progress/Internship/Resumes/Main Resume.pdf` is already tracked in git — it very likely already went public in a prior auto-commit.
- `Main Resume.md`, which I wrote this session and which has your phone number, address, and email in the header, is untracked right now but will get swept into the next auto-commit-and-push cycle (they're running every ~2 hours: last one was 13:24) unless something changes before then.
- No `.gitignore` rule currently excludes `Resumes/`, `Contacts/`, or any personal-info path.

This matters directly for what we're about to build: the automation we're designing will write recruiter names, contact info, and drafted outreach messages into this same vault, which flows through the same auto-push into the same public repo. I don't want to touch your repo visibility or gitignore rules without you deciding — this is your call, not mine to make silently.

One correction to my own gitignore edit — I preemptively excluded `List/Dossiers/`, but that's wrong given what you decided: basic auto-landed postings (company, role, link, category) carry no PII, only the on-demand Layer 5 enrichment does, and that enrichment belongs in the already-protected `Contacts/` folder, not inline in dossier files. Fixing that.

## Final build plan

Given what's decided, here's exactly what I'd build — this is the last checkpoint before I create new external infrastructure (a new GitHub repo, a scheduled Action, a repo secret), since that has real footprint beyond the vault itself.

**New repo: `gupta-builds/internship-research-loop` (private)**
Holds the Python polling script, the profile-filter config, and a `state/seen_ids.json` dedup file. Nothing here touches Claude or costs tokens.

**GitHub Actions workflow (cron, every 30-60 min)**
1. `GET` both listings.json feeds (SimplifyJobs `dev` branch, Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez `main` branch) with ETag caching.
2. Filter against a `profile.yaml` (sophomore/2028 grad, SWE/AI/data categories, Summer 2027 term, US locations — per your answer, no quant matching yet).
3. Dedup against `state/seen_ids.json` using each feed's own stable `id`.
4. For each new match: write one basic dossier file (company, role, link, category, term, deadline-ish fields, matched-profile reason — **no enrichment, no contacts, nothing sensitive**) directly into `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Dossiers/` in the **public** `gupta-builds/Jarvis` repo, via a fine-grained PAT scoped only to that repo (stored as a secret in the new automation repo, not reused from your broad `gh` CLI token).
5. `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/YYYY-MM Found.md` stops being hand-typed and becomes a Dataview query over `List/Dossiers/*`, per your answer.

**Getting it into Obsidian**
Your vault already auto-commits and pushes to `origin/master` roughly every 2 hours. If that cycle also pulls before it pushes (typical for this kind of setup, but I haven't confirmed it), the automation's commits show up locally on their own within a couple hours. I'll flag this explicitly as something to verify on the first real run rather than assume — if it turns out there's no pull step, you'd need an occasional manual `git pull`, which I can wire into that same script if so.

**Everything else stays exactly as decided:** Layer 5 (Firecrawl company research, legitimate contact discovery) and Layer 6 (Claude judgment, drafting via Mimic) only run locally, on-demand, when you promote a dossier to a real Program + Applying pair — never automatically, never touching the cloud repo, and writing into the already-gitignored `Resumes/`/`Contacts/` paths.

1. **`ingestion/` + `core/` + filter/dedup tests** — run locally against saved fixture JSON, no network, no Actions, no vault writes. Prove the Junior+any-year filter and dedup logic are correct by hand first.
2. **`vault_writer/` + the four-check write gate** — write into a throwaway local vault copy, not the real Jarvis repo, until format compliance and the gate itself are verified.
3. **Wire `.github/workflows/run.yml`** — only now does it touch `gupta-builds/Jarvis` for real, starting hourly (not every 30 min) as a conservative first cadence, schema-drift detection active from the first run.
4. **Watch the weekly Run Log rollup for a full week** before trusting it unattended.
5. Repo gets created **public** (confirmed above) — removes the Actions-minutes ceiling entirely.

Sources locked in: SimplifyJobs (primary, has real Summer 2027 data despite the name), Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez (secondary, smaller but Junior-typed), zapplyjobs (conditional — only its `All student(s)` rows, scraped from the README table). # Research Loop — Implementation Plan
==The technical spec for the 24/7 discovery automation, written so a fresh Claude Code session in a separate WSL repo can build it without re-deriving anything here.== [[Internship System — Build Log]] is the retrospective record of the folder redesign; this note is the forward spec for the loop that feeds it. Obsidian stays the source of truth throughout — the automation writes into it, never replaces it.
## Correction Carried Into This Plan
Class year corrected 2026-07-16: **rising junior**, expected grad Spring 2028 (consistent — a standard 4-year timeline from a Fall 2024 start, no contradiction with the resume). Filter targets Junior-eligible **and** any-year/unrestricted postings, not sophomore-scoped ones. The HRT Sophomore worked example built last session was withdrawn as no longer eligible — see its Log entry.
## Source Verdicts (Verified, Not Assumed)
Every claim below was fetched live, not taken from the pasted Gemini/Sonnet transcript at face value — two of that transcript's claims were wrong (see Corrections below).
| Source | Machine-readable? | 2027 coverage | Junior-eligible? | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **SimplifyJobs/Summer2026-Internships** | Yes — `listings.json` on `dev` branch, live, ~30 min cadence | 278 of 14,940 entries are `terms: "Summer 2027"` today (~2%, growing as the cycle ramps) | No class-year field, general board | **Primary source.** Despite the "2026" name, it's the successor of Pitt CSC and already carries 2027 postings. |
| **Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez/underclassmen-opportunities** | Yes — `listings.json` on `main`, live | No confirmed Summer 2027 season tagging yet | 17 of 112 entries (15%) explicitly `target_year: Junior (3rd year)` | **Secondary source.** Small volume but the most precisely class-typed feed that still includes you. |
| **zapplyjobs/underclassmen-internships** | No — README table only, plain markdown, no images/badges in cells (confirmed parseable) | Not date-typed | **None** — every Year column value is Freshman/Sophomore/"All student(s)", zero literal "Junior" rows | **Conditional source.** Scrape only rows tagged `All student(s)` — those are junior-inclusive despite the repo's underclassmen framing. Freshman/Sophomore-only rows are noise for this filter. |
| LuisaE/opportunities | No | Dead since Dec 2023 | N/A | Dropped. |
| interviewstreet/hiring-agent | N/A — not a listings repo, it's a resume-scoring tool | N/A | N/A | Not a discovery source. Its *concept* (score a resume against a JD) is worth mimicking as a small local tool — see Deferred Module below — but it's not part of the discovery pipeline. |
| yangshun/tech-interview-handbook | N/A — interview-prep content | N/A | N/A | Reference only. Feeds `Interviews/Interview Questions.md` and Cheats over time, not the automation. |
| Intern Dock (interndock.com) 2027 guide | No — JS-rendered SPA, no direct posting links, funnels to a signup product | Live, last updated 2026-06-20 | N/A | Drop as an automation source. Already sits in [[Links & Interlinks]] as a manual-check bookmark; that's enough. |
### Corrections To The Gemini/Sonnet Transcript
- It claimed listings.json is "regenerated by scheduled GitHub Actions." **Wrong mechanism** — no workflow in the repo has a cron trigger; an external bot pushes directly to `dev`, and in-repo Actions only react to that push. Doesn't change the plan, the ~30-min cadence claim itself held up.
- It implicitly assumed `vanshb03/Summer2027-Internships` might be a distinct successor worth polling separately. **Confirmed it's the same repo as `vanshb03/Summer2026-Internships`, renamed in place** — not a second data source. Not polled separately; would add parsing cost (README-only, badge-based Apply links) for no new data.
- It recommended `systemd` for orchestration. **Wrong platform** — the vault machine is Windows, `systemd` doesn't exist there. Irrelevant anyway once the decision landed on GitHub Actions (cloud-hosted, platform-independent).
## Profile Filter (Layer 2)
Pure field matching against each feed's own structured schema — no LLM call, no judgment, deterministic.
```yaml
# profile.yaml
grad_year: 2028
class_year: junior
eligible_class_tags: [Junior, "3rd year", "All student(s)"]  # matched case-insensitively against class/year fields when present
accept_unrestricted: true  # postings with no class-year field at all still match
terms: ["Summer 2027"]
categories: [Software Engineering, Data Science, Machine Learning, Artificial Intelligence]
locations_allow: [United States, Remote]
exclude_terms: ["Summer 2026", "Fall 2026", "Spring 2026"]  # this cycle has passed or isn't the target
```
Match rule per source:
- **SimplifyJobs:** `terms == "Summer 2027"` AND `category` in `categories` list. No class-year field exists on this feed — every match here is accepted under `accept_unrestricted`.
- **Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez:** `target_year` array intersects `eligible_class_tags`, OR array is empty/absent (`accept_unrestricted`).
- **zapplyjobs:** README row's Year column literally equals `All student(s)` — Freshman/Sophomore-only rows are excluded outright, not just deprioritized.
## Dedup (Layer 3)
Both JSON feeds carry a stable `id` — use it directly as the primary key (`src:<id>`). zapplyjobs has no ID, so its dedup key is a content hash of normalized company+role+link, same fallback pattern as the original transcript proposed. State lives in `state/seen_ids.json`, committed to the automation repo after every run — small, git-diffable, easy to inspect by hand if something looks wrong.
## Closed-Loop Write Gate (Layer 4)
==A dossier is only written into Jarvis if it passes every check below — this is the "test to verify we're ready to push" you asked for.== Fail any check → the item is logged as rejected (see Run Log) and never touches the vault. This is fail-closed by design: a broken pipeline should produce nothing, not garbage.
1. **Required fields present:** `company`, `title`, `url`, `source`, `uid` all non-empty.
2. **URL liveness:** a single HEAD request to `url` returns 2xx/3xx, not 404/410. Cheap, catches stale/pulled postings before they land in your List.
3. **Not a duplicate:** `uid` not already in `state/seen_ids.json`.
4. **Format compliance:** the generated markdown is built from a fixed template (never free-form text), so compliance with the vault's zero-blank-line rule and frontmatter conventions ([[Jarvis Writing and Formatting]]) is true by construction — the validator still checks it mechanically (no blank line between frontmatter close and title, no duplicate YAML keys, all required frontmatter fields present) as a regression guard, not a creative judgment call.
Only after all four pass: write `List/Dossiers/<uid-safe-slug>.md`, add `uid` to the seen-set, commit both in the same push.
### Dossier Note Shape
```yaml

No prose beyond that one line — this is Layer 4, intentionally thin. Enrichment is Layer 5, on-demand only.
## Self-Improving Test Design
"Self-improving" here means test-driven, not autonomously learning — a false-positive filter match doesn't get fixed by the pipeline itself, it gets fixed by you or Claude reading the Run Log, then that fix becomes a permanent regression test. Concretely:
- **On every push:** a pytest suite validates the filter logic against known fixture listings (hand-picked examples of should-match and should-reject cases per source), the dedup function's collision behavior, and the write-gate's four checks individually.
- **On every scheduled run, before touching the feeds:** a schema-drift check — fetch one real entry from each source and confirm the expected keys are still present. If a key vanished or renamed (upstream repos change their schema without warning), the run halts and writes nothing rather than silently producing malformed dossiers.
- **On schema-drift or write-gate failure:** the workflow opens a GitHub issue in the automation repo automatically (`gh issue create`, free, no external service) summarizing what broke. This is the feedback signal that drives the next fix — not automatic, but immediate and specific instead of a silent multi-week drift nobody notices.
## Run Log
Two tiers, matching "Obsidian is the source of truth, not a noise dump":
- **Raw, per-run:** `logs/runs.jsonl` in the automation repo — one line per run: timestamp, per-source fetch count, filter-match count, new-vs-already-seen count, write-gate rejections with reasons, errors. Cheap, git-committed, never touches Obsidian.
- **Rollup, weekly:** a short auto-generated note, `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Run Log.md`, appended (not rewritten) with one line per week: dossiers written, rejections by reason, any halted runs. This is the one piece of automation output meant for you to actually read.
## GitHub Actions Minutes
Confirmed current limits: 2,000 free minutes/month on private repos (Free plan), **unlimited standard-runner minutes on public repos**. At an every-30-min cadence (48 runs/day), a private repo hits budget if any run averages over ~1.4 minutes (checkout + fetch + filter + write + push) — plausible to exceed given three sources. **Recommendation: make the automation repo public.** The code itself (HTTP polling + a profile filter stating "CS junior, SWE/AI interests") isn't sensitive — it's less revealing than the resume already on your portfolio — and public removes the minutes ceiling entirely rather than requiring cadence tuning to stay under it. The Jarvis vault repo (destination) is a separate concern, already handled via `.gitignore` — see [[Internship System — Build Log]].
## Deferred Module: Resume Grader
`interviewstreet/hiring-agent`'s concept (score a resume against a JD) is worth a small local mimic, not the actual tool — it's an LLM-scoring product, not something to depend on. Scope: a keyword-overlap script (JD text in, `Resumes/Main Resume.md`'s tagged bullets out, ranked by tag match) that runs locally when tailoring a resume for a specific promoted program — a Layer 6 tool, not part of the discovery loop. Not built this phase; noted here so it isn't lost.
## Repo Structure
```
internship-research-loop/
├── ingestion/
│   ├── sources.py          # fetch SimplifyJobs + Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez JSON, zapplyjobs README
│   └── normalize.py        # map each source's raw shape to one internal Listing dataclass
├── core/
│   ├── profile.yaml         # the filter config above
│   ├── filter.py            # Layer 2
│   └── identity.py          # Layer 3 — compute_uid()
├── vault_writer/
│   ├── templates/dossier.md.j2
│   ├── validate.py           # Layer 4 — the four-check write gate
│   └── writer.py              # renders + writes into the Jarvis repo checkout, idempotent on uid
├── tests/
│   ├── fixtures/              # known should-match / should-reject listings per source
│   ├── test_filter.py
│   ├── test_identity.py
│   ├── test_validate.py
│   └── test_schema_drift.py
├── state/
│   └── seen_ids.json
├── logs/
│   └── runs.jsonl
└── .github/workflows/
    ├── run.yml                # scheduled: fetch → filter → dedup → validate → write → push
    └── test.yml                 # on push: pytest
```
## Build Order
1. `ingestion/` + `core/` + `tests/test_filter.py` + `test_identity.py` first, run locally against saved fixture JSON — no GitHub Actions, no vault writes yet, until the filter/dedup logic is proven correct by hand.
2. `vault_writer/` + `test_validate.py` next, writing into a **throwaway local test vault copy**, not the real Jarvis repo, until the write-gate and formatting compliance are verified.
3. Only then wire `.github/workflows/run.yml` to write into the real `gupta-builds/Jarvis` repo, starting at a conservative cadence (hourly), with the schema-drift check active from the first scheduled run.
4. Watch the Run Log rollup for at least one full week before trusting it unattended — this is the "tested in detail before we rely on it" requirement.
## Explicit Non-Goals (Carried Forward)
No CAPTCHA-bypass, cookie-injection, or stealth-browser automation against LinkedIn or any login-walled platform — same position as the prior Sonnet session reviewed this conversation. Contact discovery, when it happens (Layer 5, on-demand), uses public company pages, GitHub org members, engineering-blog bylines, and pattern-inferred email + MX validation only.

## Claude

**Option 1 — wire `exclude_terms` in, drop `locations_allow`.**

`exclude_terms` is cheap and unambiguous to implement: reject if `terms` contains any excluded value, full stop — no interpretation needed. It's worth keeping even though it looks redundant against today's `terms == "Summer 2027"` allowlist, because I never actually confirmed whether SimplifyJobs' `terms` field is always a scalar or can be a list on co-op/rotational postings (multi-term entries like `["Summer 2027", "Fall 2026"]` are plausible for extended internships). If it's ever a list, an allowlist alone (`any term matches`) would wave through a listing that's really a Fall 2026 rotation with an incidental Summer 2027 tag — `exclude_terms` catches that even when the allowlist doesn't. Cheap insurance, zero guessed semantics.

`locations_allow` is different in kind: the plan never specified the matching rule (substring vs. exact, how to treat "Remote (US)" vs "United States" vs "New York, NY", multi-location arrays, or zapplyjobs' rows which don't reliably carry location data at all). Wiring it in now means inventing that rule under time pressure, and getting it wrong fails silently — a too-strict match drops a real Junior-eligible US posting with no error, no log line, nothing visibly broken. That's a worse failure mode than not filtering on location yet. Layer 4 is deliberately thin and the promotion step is already the human review checkpoint — location gets added later as its own tested change once we've seen real `locations` data from a live run and can write fixtures against actual values instead of guessed ones.

Drop `locations_allow` from `profile.yaml` entirely rather than leaving it in as dead config — inert-but-present fields are exactly the kind of drift this project has been trying to avoid. Add one test fixture case for `exclude_terms` (a listing with both an allowed and an excluded term present) before calling phase 1's filter logic done.

I updated the plan note's `profile.yaml` and match-rule section to match this so it isn't just a chat answer — the vault stays the actual source of truth.

Plan note is consistent with the answer now — safe to relay to the WSL session.

I also found four things phase 3 needs that neither the plan nor the build report had nailed down: dependency pinning (unattended operation), a push-retry/rebase strategy (the vault already has its own independent auto-commit cycle hitting the same branch), the exact ordering for marking dedup state as "seen" (only after a confirmed push, never before), and confirmation that schema-drift detection, auto-issue-filing, and the run log are still unbuilt, not just unwired. Then build, as four non-optional requirements alongside the workflow itself:
1. Pin every dependency in requirements.txt to an exact version — this runs unattended for months, an upstream break shouldn't happen mid-cycle with no warning.

### Gaps Found In The Phase 3 Scope Itself (Not Yet Built, Must Be Before The Schedule Goes Live)
- **Dependency pinning.** `requirements.txt` was unpinned through phase 2 — fine for active development, not acceptable for something meant to run unattended for months. An upstream library update breaking mid-cycle is a failure mode this plan's schema-drift check was designed to catch for *data*, not for *code dependencies* — a different risk, needs its own fix (pin exact versions before the first scheduled run).
- **Push race condition, never addressed.** The Jarvis vault already has its own auto-commit-and-push cycle running locally every ~2 hours (see [[Internship System — Build Log]]). Phase 3's workflow will be a second, independent process pushing to the same `origin/master`. Neither this plan nor the phase 1-2 build has specified `git pull --rebase` before push or a retry-on-rejected-push loop — without it, the first real collision between the two processes either fails the run or, worse, force-overwrites something. Required for phase 3, not optional hardening.
- **State-update ordering, never specified.** `state/seen_ids.json` must only mark a `uid` as seen *after* a confirmed successful push to Jarvis — not before. If a run marks an item seen and then the push fails (network blip, the race condition above, anything), that dossier is silently gone forever: never in the vault, never retried, because dedup thinks it already landed. This plan's Layer 3 description didn't say this explicitly; it does now.
- **Schema-drift check, auto-filed issue, and the two-tier run log are all still unbuilt.** These were specified in this plan as part of the scheduled-run design, but nothing in the phase 1-2 report mentions them — they're phase 3 deliverables, not already-done work. Calling this out so phase 3 doesn't get treated as "just wire the workflow file to already-existing pieces."
