# Internship Research Loop — PRD

**Status:** Draft, assembled retroactively from the build session (2026-07-16 → 2026-07-17), not independently product-reviewed. This was built spec-first in conversation, not PRD-first — sections marked **NEEDS WORK** below are genuinely thin and should be expanded, not treated as complete.

## Problem

Manually checking internship-listing repos/boards for new postings that match a specific eligibility window (class year, term, category) is repetitive and easy to let slip. Postings also go stale fast — a manually-curated list rots.

## Goal

A 24/7 background process that watches known internship-listing sources, keeps only postings the user is actually eligible for, and writes a live, deduplicated, dead-link-free list into the user's Obsidian vault (Jarvis) — so the vault stays the single place the user looks, without them polling GitHub repos or README tables by hand.

## User

Single user (repo owner) — a rising junior CS student, grad Spring 2028, targeting Summer 2027 SWE/AI/data internships. Not built as a multi-tenant or general-purpose product; the profile (`core/profile.yaml`) is hardcoded to one person's eligibility criteria.

## In Scope — Built (Phases 1-3, complete and live)

- Poll three internship-listing sources on an hourly schedule (GitHub Actions cron)
- Filter each posting deterministically against a profile (class year, term, category) — no LLM judgment calls
- Deduplicate against a persistent seen-set so the same posting is never written twice
- Reject postings that fail a mechanical write gate (missing fields, dead URL, already seen, malformed output) before they ever reach the vault
- Write passing postings as thin, template-fixed dossier notes into the real Jarvis vault
- Push safely against the vault's own independent auto-commit cycle (pull-rebase + retry-once, never force-push)
- Halt and do nothing (rather than write garbage) if an upstream source's schema silently changes
- File a GitHub issue automatically when something needs a human (schema drift, push failure, a systemic write-gate rejection)
- Log every run (raw JSONL here, a human-readable weekly rollup in the vault)

Full build record, decisions, and what was verified live: see the Jarvis vault note `20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Phases 1-3 Run.md` (primary source of truth for status — this PRD summarizes it, the note has the detail).

## Explicitly Out Of Scope (Deferred, Not Forgotten)

- **Location filtering** (`locations_allow`) — dropped from the profile config rather than shipped with a guessed matching rule; real per-listing `locations` data now exists from live runs and hasn't been used yet
- **Enrichment** (company/contact research, cover-letter drafting) on a written posting — dossiers are deliberately thin (one line, no prose); enrichment is meant to happen on-demand only once a posting is manually promoted through the vault's own Internship Pipeline workflow, not automatically
- **Resume grader** — a keyword-overlap scorer for tailoring a resume against a JD; noted as a separate future local tool, not part of the discovery loop, not started
- **Tightening cadence** below hourly — deliberate: the plan's build order calls for a full week of clean, unattended runs before trusting a faster schedule
- Any login-walled scraping (LinkedIn, etc.), CAPTCHA bypass, or stealth browser automation — a hard non-goal, not a resourcing decision

## Architecture (Summary)

Deterministic pipeline, no LLM calls anywhere in the loop:

```
ingestion (fetch + normalize) → filter (profile match) → dedup (stable uid)
  → write gate (4 mechanical checks) → write to vault → push (retry-safe)
  → mark seen (only after confirmed push) → log
```

Full repo/module map, what each phase built, and why each design decision landed where it did: `20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Phases 1-3 Run.md` in the Jarvis vault, and `20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Research Loop — Implementation Plan.md` for the original spec.

## Current Status (2026-07-17)

Live and running unattended. 137 real dossiers written and confirmed deduplicated across repeated runs; hourly cron confirmed actually firing (not just configured); 87/87 tests passing; zero issues auto-filed so far. See the Phases 1-3 Run note for exact numbers and verification evidence.

## Success Metrics

**NEEDS WORK** — no metric beyond "the pipeline runs correctly and doesn't produce garbage" has been defined yet. This PRD does not yet state a target like conversion to applications, time saved vs. manual checking, or an acceptable false-negative rate (eligible postings the filter wrongly rejects). Define these before treating the loop as "done" in a product sense, not just an infra sense.

## Open Backlog

See the Phases 1-3 Run note's "What's Not Built" and "Open Questions" sections for the current, maintained list — not duplicated here to avoid the two documents drifting out of sync. As of 2026-07-17 the live items are: confirming the first real weekly-rollup write, deciding on cadence after a week of clean runs, and revisiting `locations_allow` now that real location data exists.

## Risks

**NEEDS WORK** — not yet assessed: what happens if a source repo goes fully offline or is deleted; what happens if the fine-grained PAT expires or is revoked; whether GitHub Actions' public-repo minutes policy could change. None of these have caused a real incident yet, so none have been prioritized, but none are written down as accepted risks either.

## Source Of Truth

This file plus `20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Phases 1-3 Run.md` in the Jarvis vault are the two documents to read before extending this project. If they ever disagree, the vault note is the more detailed and more recently maintained one — treat a conflict as a sign this PRD needs a sync pass, not as license to trust whichever is more convenient.

grad_year: 2028
class_year: junior
eligible_class_tags: [Junior, "3rd year", "All student(s)"]  # matched as case-insensitive substrings against target_year entries
accept_unrestricted: true  # postings with no class-year field at all still match
terms: ["Summer 2027"]
# Real category values observed on SimplifyJobs/Summer2026-Internships (dev/.github/scripts/listings.json),
# not the generic names in the original transcript — confirmed by fetching live data 2026-07-16.
categories: ["Software", "Software Engineering", "AI/ML/Data", "Data Science, AI & Machine Learning"]
# Rejects a SimplifyJobs listing outright if any of these terms are present, even
# alongside an allowed term (multi-term/rotational postings spanning both cycles).
exclude_terms: ["Summer 2026", "Fall 2026", "Spring 2026"]
# Rule built 2026-07-17 from live feed data (1216 distinct location strings), not
# guessed — see location_eligible() in core/filter.py. Permissive: no location
# data or ambiguous strings ('Multiple Locations', 'Virtual', bare 'Remote') still
# match; only affirmatively non-US listings (Canada/UK/'Remote in Germany') drop.
locations_allow: us_remote

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Layer 5 — on-demand company/contact enrichment for one promoted dossier.

Run manually at Step 2 (Commit) of the vault's Internship Pipeline workflow —
deliberately NOT called from run_pipeline.py and never automatic on dossiers
the discovery loop writes:

    FIRECRAWL_API_KEY=... python enrich.py "<path to dossier .md>"

Public sources only: company site/blog scraped via Firecrawl, GitHub org
public members, blog author bylines, pattern-inferred first.last@domain
checked against the domain's MX records (DNS-over-HTTPS — no new dependency).
Hard line per the plan: no LinkedIn, no CAPTCHA bypass, no cookie injection,
no stealth browsing, no login walls. Anything behind one gets skipped.

No LLM call anywhere — scraped text is trimmed verbatim, never summarized.
"""
import os
import re
import sys
from datetime import date
from urllib.parse import urlparse

import requests
import yaml

FIRECRAWL = "https://api.firecrawl.dev/v1"
TIMEOUT = 30


def read_dossier(text: str) -> dict:
    m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
    if not m:
        raise ValueError("no YAML frontmatter found — is this a dossier file?")
    return yaml.safe_load(m.group(1))


def replace_enrichment(text: str, section: str) -> str:
    """Idempotent: a re-run replaces the previous Enrichment section."""
    base = re.split(r"\n## Enrichment\b", text)[0].rstrip("\n")

## What I found — before touching a single webpage

I cross-referenced every dossier's stored ID against the live upstream JSON feeds (one fetch each, then a local join) rather than visiting 137 individual URLs — this alone resolved cycle-correctness, liveness, and degree-eligibility for 117 of the 137 files with zero web scraping. Here's the breakdown, then the root causes.

### SimplifyJobs (104 files)
| Filter | Count |
|---|---|
| Total | 104 |
| **Marked `active: false` upstream** — the source itself now says this posting is closed | **76** |
| Of the remaining 28 active: **grad-only** (PhD/Master's, no Bachelor's — not eligible for you at all) | 3 |
| **Genuinely valid** — Summer 2027 confirmed in real data, still active, Bachelor's-eligible | **25** |

Every single one of the 104 genuinely does carry "Summer 2027" in its real `terms` array — my initial suspicion (Caterpillar/T. Rowe Price/Oaktree/Scottish Water titles saying "2026") was a false alarm, that's just each company's internal req-naming convention, not the actual term. So SimplifyJobs' term-matching was never the problem. **Staleness was the problem — 73% of what's sitting in the folder is for a posting the source has already marked closed.**

### Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez (13 files)
- **9 UUID-based entries**: all genuinely Junior-eligible per live data (confirms these weren't false-positive matches) — but 7 of 9 are `active: false` upstream. Only 2 survive: a UNCF scholarship portal and a White House HBCU scholars program — both scholarships/programs, not internships in the narrow sense.
- **4 slug-named entries** (`google-gsoc-2026`, `[REDACTED]`, `[REDACTED]`, `[REDACTED]`): not UUID-based, can't cross-reference against the live feed the same way, but 3 of 4 literally have the wrong cycle in their own filename (`summer-2026`, `spring-2026`) — these are stale by their own name.

### zapplyjobs (20 files)
None of these are actually internship postings with a deadline. They're program/fellowship landing pages: NASA's generic portal, Forage (a job-simulation *platform*, not a posting), Google Summer of Code, MLH Fellowship (also separately duplicated via JGCL — same program, two files, because dedup is per-source, not cross-source), Outreachy, INROADS, etc. None of these have a single deadline the way a specific job req does — most are rolling or annual. **This source doesn't fit what a "dossier with a deadline" is supposed to be.**

### Root causes (so this doesn't recur)
1. **No recheck after write.** A dossier gets written once when first discovered and never revisited — if the source later marks it closed, nothing in this pipeline notices. This is the single biggest cause of the folder's bloat.
2. **`degrees` was never checked** — same class of gap as the `locations_allow` issue from Phase 4: real eligibility data exists upstream and nothing reads it.
3. **JGCL's `target_year` isn't reaching the dossier frontmatter** — the live data has real values (confirmed Junior-eligible on all 9), but every written dossier shows it empty. A write/normalize bug, not a false-positive matching bug — but it makes the notes themselves unauditable.
4. **No cross-source dedup** — the same program can land as two separate files if two sources both list it.
5. **zapplyjobs shouldn't be feeding this folder at all** — it's a resource directory, not a listings feed with deadlines.

## Before I fetch a single deadline

That work only makes sense for the **25 SimplifyJobs entries that survived this triage** (plus maybe the 2 JGCL scholarships, your call) — fetching deadlines for the other ~110 is wasted effort on files that don't belong here regardless of their deadline. I want your go-ahead before I remove/archive anything, since that's ~112 files disappearing from the vault.

Cleanup done: **137 → 27 files**, all verified against live source data, zero webpage visits needed. Recorded in the Run note and session log.

One thing left before this is actually finished: the folder will refill with the same junk on the next hourly run unless the pipeline itself gets fixed.

```
Fix the 5 root causes behind the Dossiers folder bloat found in a vault audit — read the "Vault Cleanup And Root-Cause Findings" section (dated 2026-07-18) in the Run note first:

Context: 110 of 137 dossiers were just deleted directly from the vault (76 SimplifyJobs closed upstream, 3 grad-only, 11 JGCL closed/wrong-cycle, 20 zapplyjobs — wrong content type entirely). 27 survive. This deletion happened outside your pipeline, by cross-referencing live upstream JSON against stored UUIDs — state/seen_ids.json still marks all 137 as seen, which is correct (don't rewrite the deleted ones, they're closed or wrong-fit). But nothing stops the same bloat from recurring unless these five ship:

1. Post-write liveness recheck. Nothing currently notices when a written dossier's posting closes upstream. Add a periodic pass (daily, separate from the hourly discovery cron — no reason to recheck every posting every hour) that re-fetches each source's live data, checks `active` status for every dossier currently present in the vault (check actual file existence in 10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Dossiers/, not just seen_ids.json — the two are no longer the same set after today's manual deletion), and removes any dossier whose posting is now inactive. Log every removal the same way rejections are logged today.

2. `degrees` eligibility. profile.yaml and core/filter.py never check the `degrees` field. Add it: reject a match if `degrees` is non-empty and doesn't include "Bachelor's" (PhD-only/Master's-only listings aren't eligible for this profile). Same permissive-by-default pattern as locations_allow — empty/missing degrees data still passes.

3. JGCL target_year not reaching the dossier. Confirmed via live cross-reference: all 9 UUID-based JGCL dossiers audited today genuinely had "Junior (3rd year)" in their real upstream target_year data, but every written dossier shows target_year empty. This is a normalize.py or vault_writer bug, not a false-positive match — find where the field gets dropped between ingestion and the rendered frontmatter, fix it, add a regression test using real JGCL data (not synthetic).

4. Cross-source dedup. The same program can land as two separate files if two sources both list it (MLH Fellowship appeared via both zapplyjobs and JGCL before today's cleanup). Add a secondary dedup check on normalized company+title, independent of the per-source uid, before writing.

5. Remove zapplyjobs as a source entirely. Confirmed today: its entries are program/resource landing pages (Forage, NASA's generic portal, fellowship pages), not individual deadline-bearing postings — structurally the wrong fit for this pipeline. # SimplifyJobs: delete everything NOT in the valid set

  case "$f" in
    "jose-gael-cruz-lopez-792fbc5d-41af-4722-8800-22eb447d1602.md"|"jose-gael-cruz-lopez-f90c8bcc-5c61-4def-8342-f180485d9a74.md")
      ;;
    *) rm -f "$f" ;;
  esac
done

# zapplyjobs: remove all
rm -f zapplyjobs-*.md

echo "=== remaining files ==="
ls *.md | wc -l
ls *.md`

· Item 4 design — cross-source dedup as a fifth write-gate check ('cross_source_duplicate') on normalized company+title. The existing-keys set comes from scanning actual dossier files in the checkout at run start (consistent with the recheck's file-truth principle), plus items written earlier in the same run; first source in write order wins (SimplifyJobs → JGCL). Rejections log like any other gate check, classified routine. OK? → Yes, as designed (Recommended)
· Item 3's stated bug (JGCL target_year empty in dossiers) doesn't reproduce — git history shows all 13 written JGCL dossiers had it populated. ## Claude

Let me verify that specific claim myself rather than just accept it — it directly contradicts what I read from the files earlier, and this project's whole discipline has been "check, don't relay."

All four are correct — and one of them caught a real mistake of mine. Verified independently rather than taking either side's word for it.

**Items 1, 2, and 4: agree, all well-reasoned.**
- The "recheck + active filter" expansion is the right call — 71/103 already-inactive at discovery time means most of the bloat isn't decay-after-write, it's writing dead-on-arrival listings. Fixing only the recheck (my original ask) would've left the bigger half of the problem untouched.
- The mass-deletion brake is the best addition in this batch, and I didn't ask for it. A recheck that can now *delete* vault content needs a stronger circuit breaker than a rejection gate that only refuses to *write* — a transient upstream glitch (empty/malformed feed) could otherwise wipe the folder. Exactly the same fail-closed instinct as the schema-drift halt from Phase 3, correctly extended to a more dangerous capability.
- Cross-source dedup as a 5th write-gate check, file-truth at run start, routine classification — all consistent with existing patterns, not reinvented. Agree as designed.

**Item 3: they're right, I was wrong.** I checked git history myself just now on one of the surviving JGCL dossiers — one commit, ever, and `target_year` was populated with real data (Freshman/Sophomore/Junior/Senior) from that very first write. My original "target_year not persisting" claim doesn't hold up. What actually happened: my own audit grep (`grep -E "^target_year:"`) only captures a YAML key's own line, not the indented list items below it — `target_year:` followed by a multi-line array read as "empty" to my pattern when it wasn't. A tooling artifact on my end, not a pipeline bug. `terms: []` being empty on every JGCL entry was real, though (single-line inline array, my grep caught that one correctly) — and that's exactly what `season` never being mapped would produce, and exactly what let the 4 wrong-cycle dossiers through. Fix `season`, not a phantom `target_year` bug.

I corrected the vault record — it had my wrong claim written into it as fact.

All four designs are good — go ahead and build them.

## TASK 1 — Full codebase review

Read every module, not just the ones touched most recently. ## TASK 2 — Verify the workflow is actually working, not reported as working

## TASK 3 — Re-audit all 27 current dossiers against exactly three criteria

Read every file in `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Dossiers/` in the vault. For each, check:
1. **Timing** — Summer 2027, or genuinely December 2026-January 2027. For anything currently matched via a "Winter 2026"/"Winter 2027" term bucket, don't assume that maps to Dec-Jan — check the actual posting page for real stated dates before trusting the term label.

3. **OPT eligibility** — per the permissive-by-default pattern in the Run note's Phase 6 section: exclude only on an explicit US-citizenship requirement, a security-clearance requirement, or an explicit "OPT/CPT not accepted" statement. Check RTX, Northrop Grumman, and the Palantir "US Government" role specifically — high-probability citizenship-gated. Everything else defaults to eligible unless the actual posting text says otherwise.

Remove anything that fails any of the three. Report what was removed and why, same evidence standard as every prior pass — cite what you actually read, not an inference.

## TASK 4 — Give every surviving dossier real content

No longer deliberately thin. For each survivor: Firecrawl-fetch the stored `url`, pull the substantive sections (role summary, requirements/qualifications, comp if stated) — verbatim/structural extraction, trimmed to drop page chrome, not a summary. Firecrawl's own structured-extraction features are fine to use here (their infrastructure, not Claude/Anthropic usage — doesn't touch this loop's "no LLM call" principle, which was about not spending Claude tokens on an unattended background process). One fetch per posting can reasonably cover both this and the Task 3 OPT check where the posting page itself has the relevant signal.

## TASK 5 — Codify all three criteria into the pipeline, not just today's cleanup

- Timing: confirm the real term/date semantics for the Dec-Jan window before adding it to `profile.yaml` — don't guess "Winter 2026" means what you think it means.

# Internship Research Loop — PRD

**Status:** Verified against live repo/GitHub state on 2026-07-17 (git log, test run, `gh api`/`gh run list` — not assumed from memory). Still not independently product-reviewed; this was built spec-first in conversation, not PRD-first. Read this file alone for orientation — it does not require the Jarvis vault to make sense. For build history, decisions, and how each number below was verified, see `20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Phases 1-3 Run.md` in the Jarvis vault; it is not required reading to pick this project up, only to understand *why* it looks this way.

## Problem

Manually checking internship-listing repos/boards for new postings that match a specific eligibility window (class year, term, category) is repetitive and easy to let slip. Postings also go stale fast — a manually-curated list rots.

## Goal

A 24/7 background process that watches known internship-listing sources, keeps only postings the user is actually eligible for, and writes a live, deduplicated, dead-link-free list into the user's Obsidian vault (Jarvis) — so the vault stays the single place the user looks, without them polling GitHub repos or README tables by hand.

## User

Single user (repo owner) — a rising junior CS student, grad Spring 2028, targeting Summer 2027 SWE/AI/data internships. Not built as a multi-tenant or general-purpose product; the profile (`core/profile.yaml`) is hardcoded to one person's eligibility criteria.

## In Scope — Built (Phases 1-3, complete and live)

- Poll three internship-listing sources on an hourly schedule (GitHub Actions cron)
- Filter each posting deterministically against a profile (class year, term, category) — no LLM judgment calls
- Deduplicate against a persistent seen-set (`state/seen_ids.json`) so the same posting is never written twice
- Reject postings that fail a mechanical write gate (missing fields, dead URL, already seen, malformed output) before they ever reach the vault
- Write passing postings as thin, template-fixed dossier notes into the real Jarvis vault
- Push safely against the vault's own independent auto-commit cycle (pull-rebase + retry-once, never force-push)
- Halt and do nothing (rather than write garbage) if an upstream source's schema silently changes shape
- File a GitHub issue automatically on schema drift, a push failure, or a systemic write-gate rejection
- Log every run (raw JSONL here, a human-readable weekly rollup meant for the vault — see Open Backlog)

## Explicitly Out Of Scope (Deferred, Not Forgotten)

- **Location filtering** (`locations_allow`) — dropped from the profile config rather than shipped with a guessed matching rule; real per-listing `locations` data now exists from live runs and hasn't been used yet
- **Enrichment** (company/contact research, cover-letter drafting) on a written posting — dossiers are deliberately thin (one line, no prose); enrichment is meant to happen on-demand only once a posting is manually promoted through the vault's own Internship Pipeline workflow, not automatically
- **Resume grader** — a keyword-overlap scorer for tailoring a resume against a JD; noted as a separate future local tool, not part of the discovery loop, not started
- **Tightening cadence** below hourly — deliberate: the plan's build order calls for a full week of clean, unattended runs before trusting a faster schedule
- Any login-walled scraping (LinkedIn, etc.), CAPTCHA bypass, or stealth browser automation — a hard non-goal, not a resourcing decision

## Architecture (Summary)

Deterministic pipeline, no LLM calls anywhere in the loop:

```
ingestion (fetch + normalize) → filter (profile match) → dedup (stable uid)
  → write gate (4 mechanical checks) → write to vault → push (retry-safe)
  → mark seen (only after confirmed push) → log
```

Repo layout: `ingestion/` (fetch + normalize each source), `core/` (`filter.py`, `identity.py`, `profile.yaml`, plus phase-3 additions `schema_drift.py`, `git_ops.py`, `run_log.py`), `vault_writer/` (template + four-check `validate.py` + `writer.py`), `run_pipeline.py` (orchestrates all of it), `tests/` (one file per module, 87 tests total), `state/seen_ids.json`, `logs/runs.jsonl`, `.github/workflows/run.yml` (hourly cron + manual dispatch) and `test.yml` (pytest on push). Full module-by-module detail and design rationale: the Phases 1-3 Run note.

## Phase 6 Decision — Three Hard Criteria, Dossier Content, OPT (2026-07-18)
The final scope for this pipeline: exactly three eligibility criteria, and dossiers stop being thin.
### The three criteria
1. **Timing** — Summer 2027, or December 2026-January 2027 (a narrow winter window, not the broader "Winter 2026"/"Winter 2027" term buckets already in the data — those need checking against real posting dates before being trusted as this window, not assumed equivalent).
2. **Location** — United States. `locations_allow: us_remote` already exists and is live; re-verify it's actually enforcing "in the US," not just "not affirmatively foreign" (the current design is permissive-by-default, which was the right call for false-negative avoidance but needs re-examination against this stricter framing).
3. **OPT eligibility — new, not previously in scope.** No structured feed carries this; it has to be inferred from the posting/company. Working understanding, not verified by an immigration authority — confirm the semantics before hard-coding them:
   - OPT (Optional Practical Training) is work authorization the international student already holds via their own F-1 status — it is **not** the same as H-1B visa sponsorship, which is a separate, heavier employer commitment. A posting saying "no visa sponsorship" is often still fine for OPT; it usually means no H-1B.
   - The real exclusion signal is different: **US-citizenship-only requirements and security-clearance requirements effectively exclude OPT**, and some postings explicitly exclude OPT/CPT by name. The dossier set already has concrete examples worth checking first: RTX/Raytheon, Northrop Grumman, and Palantir's "US Government" role — all defense/government-adjacent, all plausibly citizenship-gated.
   - Recommended pattern, consistent with every other filter in this pipeline: **permissive by default, exclude only on an explicit negative signal** (citizenship-required, clearance-required, or an explicit "OPT/CPT not accepted" statement) — not a guessed allowlist of "OPT-friendly companies," which would silently reject good matches with no error, the exact failure mode `locations_allow` was designed to avoid.
   - `20_Progress/Internship/Companies giving OPT & CPT.md` is **not usable as-is** — checked its actual content: despite the filename, it's a stale October 2025 brainstorm of FAANG postings with no OPT/CPT information anywhere in it. Either rebuild it into a real, incrementally-researched company-eligibility cache (checked once per company via Firecrawl, cached, not re-fetched every run) or don't rely on it — don't assume it's a usable seed list.
### Dossier content — no longer deliberately thin
The one-line dossier body was the right call for the discovery-only phase; it isn't anymore now that a human is meant to actually screen these. Each surviving dossier needs the real posting's key content — role summary, requirements/qualifications, comp if stated — pulled via Firecrawl from the stored `url` and trimmed to the substantive sections, not the full page chrome. This is verbatim/structural extraction, not summarization — Firecrawl's own structured-extraction features are fine to use here (they run on Firecrawl's infrastructure, not Claude/Anthropic usage, so they don't violate this loop's "no LLM call" principle, which was specifically about not spending Claude tokens on a 24/7 background process). One fetch per posting can reasonably serve both this and the OPT signal check.
### What still needs deciding, not assumed here
Whether the OPT/content-fetch work runs at discovery time (every new match, ongoing Firecrawl cost) or stays promotion-triggered like Layer 5 (cheaper, more manual) is not decided in this note — it's the first thing the next build pass needs to settle, with real cost numbers, not a guess.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Deterministic, zero/near-zero-LLM-cost 24/7 discovery pipeline: ingestion → filter → dedup → write-gate → write-to-vault → push → mark-seen → log, explicitly "no LLM call anywhere in this loop" (protecting Claude/Anthropic token spend specifically — Firecrawl's own internal LLM-based extraction is explicitly NOT a violation of this principle since it's not Claude usage).
   - GitHub Actions cron scheduling (hourly discovery `run.yml`, daily 06:30 UTC `recheck.yml`, `test.yml` on push); public repo chosen specifically to avoid the 2,000 free-minutes/month private-repo Actions cap.
   - Fail-closed design philosophy applied consistently: four-check write gate (required_fields, url_liveness, not_duplicate, format_compliance, later a 5th `cross_source_duplicate` check), schema-drift halt-before-fetch, and a "mass-deletion brake" on the new `recheck.py` (halts and files a GitHub issue rather than deleting more than half the vault's dossiers if a feed glitches).
   - "File-truth principle": recheck and cross-source-dedup both trust actual files present in the vault checkout over any internal cache/state file, since the manual cleanup permanently diverged `state/seen_ids.json` from the real vault contents.
   - Permissive-by-default / exclude-on-explicit-negative-signal filter design pattern, applied consistently across `locations_allow` (US-state-suffix and full-state-name matching before a foreign-token denylist; ambiguous/missing data passes), `degrees_allow` (`["Bachelor's"]`, permissive on empty), and now proposed for OPT eligibility (exclude only on citizenship-required/clearance-required/explicit-OPT-exclusion signals, not a guessed allowlist).
   - Fine-grained GitHub PAT (`JARVIS_PUSH_TOKEN`) scoped only to `gupta-builds/Jarvis` with `contents: write`, stored as a repo secret — never the broad `gh` CLI token.
   - `commit_and_push_with_retry()` in `core/git_ops.py` — pull-rebase before push, retry-once, never force-push — built specifically because the Jarvis vault has its own independent auto-commit-and-push cycle (~every 2 hours) hitting the same `origin/master`.
   - Seen-state-only-after-confirmed-push ordering in `run_pipeline.py` (prevents silently losing a dossier if a push fails after being marked seen).
   - Two-tier run logging: raw JSONL (`logs/runs.jsonl`, `logs/rechecks.jsonl`) in the automation repo + a weekly human-readable rollup meant for `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Run Log.md` in the vault (time-gated, fires Sunday 23:00 UTC, first real firing was still pending as of the last check).
   - OPT (Optional Practical Training) vs. H-1B visa sponsorship — an important immigration-status distinction I reasoned through carefully and flagged as not authoritatively verified.
   - WSL-to-Windows bridge pattern for inspecting the automation repo from this Windows-based Claude Code session: `wsl.exe -e bash -lc '<command>'` (must use single-quote wrapping to avoid quoting failures observed with double-quoted inline scripts).
   - `jq` + `awk`/`comm` bulk local/live-data joins (used to cross-reference 104+13 dossier UUIDs against live upstream JSON in a handful of cheap HTTP fetches instead of 137 individual webpage visits) — established as the efficient verification pattern for this kind of large-scale audit.
   - Obsidian vault conventions already established and must be respected: zero blank lines except after a callout/blockquote before prose; frontmatter field discipline; Dataview-generated `List/YYYY-MM Found.md` monthly views over `List/Dossiers/*.md` (not hand-typed tables); "Obsidian is the source of truth" — the WSL repo's PRD.md and the vault's `Phases 1-3 Run.md` / `Research Loop — Implementation Plan.md` are the two documents that must stay in sync as records of ground truth, with the vault note treated as authoritative when they disagree.

# Internship Research Loop — Source of Truth
==This is the complete, closed statement of everything the internship research loop was built to do — the target scope, in one place, final form. It is not a build report and does not claim any of this is verified working right now — [[20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Phases 1-3 Run]] carries the dated evidence log across six phases, and an independent audit (2026-07-19) is the next step to confirm what's actually true against this list. This note exists because the scope grew across six phases in two other notes and no single page stated the finished aim.== [[20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Research Loop — Implementation Plan]] is the original forward spec this was built against.
## What This System Is
A GitHub Actions workflow in the public repo `gupta-builds/internship-research-loop` polls internship-listing sources on a schedule, filters them against a fixed profile with zero Claude/Anthropic LLM calls anywhere in the loop, and writes the ones that pass into `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Dossiers/` in this vault — deterministic field-matching and mechanical checks only, by design, so a 24/7 background process never spends Claude tokens.
## The Complete Scope — Every Component Aimed For
### Discovery Pipeline (Phases 1–3)
Ingestion from SimplifyJobs and Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez `listings.json` feeds (zapplyjobs was a third source, later removed entirely — see Phase 5 below); a profile filter (`core/filter.py`) matching rising-junior eligibility, target terms, and category against each source's own schema; a stable `compute_uid()` per source for dedup against `state/seen_ids.json`; a four-check fail-closed write gate (`required_fields`, `url_liveness`, `not_duplicate`, `format_compliance`) before anything touches the vault; pinned dependencies, push-retry-with-rebase against the vault's own independent auto-commit cycle, seen-state marked only after a confirmed push, a pre-fetch schema-drift halt, and a two-tier run log (raw JSONL + a weekly Obsidian rollup) — all four specified as non-optional for unattended scheduled operation, not just wiring.
### Enrichment and Resume Tooling (Phase 4 Scope Decision)
Two tools originally scoped as separate future utilities were pulled into this project: Layer 5 company/contact enrichment (Firecrawl scrape of the company site/blog, contacts from public sources only — GitHub org members, blog bylines, pattern-inferred email validated by MX record — appended to a dossier's `## Enrichment` section), staying promotion-triggered (run manually at [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]] Step 2, never automatic on discovery); and Layer 6, a local keyword-overlap resume grader scoring `Resumes/Main Resume.md`'s tagged bullets against a pasted JD — zero network, zero LLM, a standalone tool.
### Root-Cause Hardening (Phase 5)
Five gaps found during a manual 137→27 dossier cleanup were meant to become permanent code, not a one-time fix: a post-write liveness recheck (`recheck.py` + a separate daily cron, distinct from hourly discovery, with a mass-deletion brake that halts and files an issue rather than deleting more than half the vault), a `degrees_allow: ["Bachelor's"]` gate, JGCL's `season` field mapped into the term-matching path (it was never read before), a fifth write-gate check for cross-source duplicates (the same program landing as separate files from two feeds), and zapplyjobs removed from the pipeline entirely as structurally the wrong fit (its entries are program/resource pages, not deadline-bearing postings).
### The Three Hard Criteria, Real Dossier Content, Permanent Codification (Phase 6)
The final scope addition: dossiers stop being auto-generated one-liners and carry the actual posting's substantive content (role, requirements, comp — verbatim/structural extraction via Firecrawl, not summarization); every survivor is re-screened against exactly three criteria, detailed below; and all three criteria get written into `profile.yaml`/`filter.py` permanently rather than applied as a one-time manual pass.
## The Three Hard Criteria — Final, Definitive Form
1. **Timing** — Summer 2027, or genuinely December 2026–January 2027 (not the full "Winter" term bucket by label alone; a posting's real stated dates should confirm the narrow window where the feed itself doesn't publish months).
2. **Location** — United States. Permissive-by-default is the deliberate design: an affirmative US signal always passes, an affirmative foreign signal always rejects, and anything ambiguous (no location data, "Remote," "Multiple Locations") passes rather than being guessed away — a false negative here silently loses a real match with no error to show for it, which is worse than a rare false positive a human catches on read.
3. **OPT eligibility** — checked per posting, not per company (one company can differ on this axis role-to-role). OPT (Optional Practical Training) is F-1 work authorization the international student already holds; it is not H-1B sponsorship, and "no visa sponsorship" on a posting is not itself an exclusion signal. Exclude only on an explicit negative signal: a US-citizenship/US-person requirement, a security-clearance requirement, or an explicit "OPT/CPT not accepted" statement — never a guessed allowlist of "OPT-friendly companies," the same failure mode the location filter was designed to avoid.
All three share one design principle, applied consistently since Phase 2's `locations_allow` decision: **permissive by default, exclude only on an explicit negative signal.** A wrong guess in either direction is not symmetric here — a false exclusion loses a real opportunity silently; a false inclusion costs one human screening read.
## Explicit Non-Goals
No CAPTCHA-bypass, cookie-injection, or stealth-browser automation against LinkedIn or any login-walled platform, at any phase. No Claude/Anthropic LLM call anywhere in the unattended discovery/recheck/OPT-check path — Firecrawl's own extraction runs on Firecrawl's infrastructure and was never in scope for this restriction, since the principle is about not spending Claude tokens on a background process, not about avoiding LLMs categorically. Contact discovery (Layer 5) uses public sources only — no LinkedIn scraping there either.
## What "Closing The Loop" Means Here
Every component above has a claimed-built status recorded with dated evidence in [[20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Phases 1-3 Run]]. This note does not re-assert those claims as independently confirmed — that confirmation is the explicit job of the audit prompt logged in the session log on 2026-07-19, run in a fresh session precisely so the check isn't performed by the same session that wrote the claims. Read this note for *what the system was supposed to become*; read the audit for *what it actually is* as of the date it runs.

You're currently using two GitHub-hosted community lists (SimplifyJobs, Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez/underclassmen-opportunities) that feed your hourly pipeline. Here's a detailed assessment of reliability, coverage, and what could be missing:

Current Resources: Reliability & Coverage

SimplifyJobs (14,907 fetched → 36 match)
- ✅ Reliable: Actively maintained (new PRs almost daily), JSON schema stable, high-quality student curation
- ✅ Coverage: Broad—aggregates from companies + Greenhouse/Lever/Bamboo APIs, but biased toward well-known/tech-popular firms
- ⚠️ Gap: Mid-size + niche companies underrepresented (if a company doesn't actively post to SimplifyJobs, SimplifyJobs won't have it)

Jose-Gael-Cruz-Lopez (112 fetched → 5 match)
- ⚠️ Reliability: Minimal maintenance (last commit ~July 2025), small volunteer-run list
- ✅ Focus: Explicitly targets underclassmen (your profile), so high relevance when present
- ⚠️ Coverage: ~112 is tiny; many legit internships never reach this list

Removed: zapplyjobs
- Correctly removed (you found it was program landing pages, not deadline postings)

---
What You're Likely Missing

The biggest blind spot: Internships not curated by SimplifyJobs—these fall into:

1. Mid-size + deep-tech companies (not part of SimplifyJobs' feed):
  - Defense/aerospace (Palantir, Anduril, Lockheed, Northrop) — though you already filter these
  - Robotics (Boston Dynamics, Clearpath, Stäubli) — pure company sites
  - Infrastructure/energy (Waymo, Commonwealth Fusion Systems, TerraPower)
  - Fintech/crypto (Jane Street, Citadel, Rippling, etc.)
2. Regional/non-venture-backed firms that post directly to their careers page:
  - Hardware companies (most don't syndicate broadly)
  - Government contractors (Raytheon, Johns Hopkins APL, MITRE)
  - Academic spin-offs (Stanford, MIT-originated startups)
3. Seasonal posting patterns:
  - Some companies post Fall internships only in Aug-Sept, Winter only in Oct-Nov
  - SimplifyJobs/JGCL lag by 1-2 weeks (they curate manually)
  - Direct company sites have the truth-time source

---
Direct Company Career Pages vs. Hiring Platforms

Your instinct is right. Here's why:

┌───────────────┬─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
│  Source Type  │       Latency       │     Accuracy      │      Data Richness      │    Reliability    │
├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
│ Company       │ Real-time           │                   │                         │ Depends on        │
│ career site   │ (source-of-truth)   │ 100% (canonical)  │ Full content            │ company (mostly   │
│               │                     │                   │                         │ stable)           │
├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
│               │                     │ 95% (human        │ Partial                 │                   │
│ SimplifyJobs  │ 1-2 weeks lag       │ curation error)   │ (title/company/terms    │ Very high         │
│               │                     │                   │ only)                   │                   │
├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
│ LinkedIn      │ 1-2 weeks lag       │ 85% (duplicates,  │ Partial                 │ High, but closed  │
│               │                     │ stale jobs)       │                         │ source            │
├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
│               │                     │ 90%               │                         │ High, but         │
│ Handshake     │ 1-2 weeks lag       │ (institutional    │ Rich                    │ login-walled      │
│               │                     │ curation)         │                         │                   │
└───────────────┴─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴───────────────────┘

For your use case: Direct company career pages win on:
- ✅ No lag (you fetch at discovery time)
- ✅ No duplication (each company has one canonical job posting)
- ✅ Full content already (job description, requirements, comp, application deadline)
- ✅ Direct application (no platform intermediary)

The tradeoff: You need a list of which companies to monitor, plus parsing logic for their (many different) career-site schemas. SimplifyJobs solves this for you already.

---
Testing Step-1: Discovery Coverage Audit

Here's what I'd recommend to verify you're not missing internships:

A. Seed companies from your existing vault

- Extract all 20 dossiers → unique companies
- For each, manually check their careers page: do they have a Summer 2027 / Winter 2027 SWE/data/AI internship posted?
- Count how many are not in SimplifyJobs/JGCL
- → This tells you the coverage gap empirically

B. Expand the company seed

Use a lightweight source to discover companies you should monitor:
1. AngelList Talent (https://www.angel.co/company/search): Search "internship" + filter by stage/location — gives you a list of companies in hiring mode, many pre-SimplifyJobs
2. Crunchbase (free tier): Search "actively hiring" + "SWE/data/AI roles"
3. Industry lists: e.g., YC companies (ycombinator.com/companies), AWS partners, etc.
4. LinkedIn advanced search (read-only, no scraping): Company search by headcount/stage, then cross-ref careers pages

C. Build a monitored company seed file (lightweight approach)

Instead of scraping 14k listings, maintain a companies_to_monitor.json:

[
  {
    "name": "Anthropic",
    "career_url": "https://www.anthropic.com/careers",
    "jd_pattern": "/opportunities/{id}",
    "internship_terms": ["Summer 2027", "Winter 2027"],
    "last_checked": null
  },
  ...
]

Then:
- Option 1 (manual, reliable): Periodically fetch each careers page, extract job IDs, deduplicate against SimplifyJobs
- Option 2 (semi-automated): Use Firecrawl (which you already have API access to) to scrape the careers pages, extract job listings
- Option 3 (hybrid, low-cost): SimplifyJobs + JGCL as your primary feed, plus a monthly manual audit of 20-50 high-priority companies

D. Quantify the gap

Run this diagnostic:
1. Log all companies in current SimplifyJobs/JGCL feeds into a set simplified_set
2. Manually check 50 high-growth companies' careers pages (e.g., top YC companies 2024-2025)
3. Count how many have live internship postings not in simplified_set
4. Extrapolate to total opportunity loss

---
Should You Build Better Scraping?

Not yet. Here's the ladder:

1. Before scraping: Test the SimplifyJobs + JGCL + manual company-site audit workflow for 2-3 weeks
  - You're already at 20 vault dossiers + 36 new matches per feed
  - See if manual company-site checks capture internships you'd otherwise miss
  - Track the "false negatives" (companies you know you'd want to work at, but no internship listed)
2. If gaps are real: Build a lightweight company monitor
  - Start with a companies.json list of 50-100 high-priority targets
  - Use Firecrawl (you have API key) to fetch their /careers pages weekly
  - Extract job IDs via regex/heuristics, deduplicate against your vault
  - This costs ~1-2 Firecrawl calls per company per week (~$0.10 total)
3. Only if you hit >10% miss rate: Consider a scraper
  - But parsing 50+ different career-page layouts is the hard part
  - Firecrawl (markdown extraction) likely handles it better than a custom scraper```. Now the session log entry, then I'll give you the full synthesis.

Straight answer to your first question: **no, this wasn't actually closed.** The discovery half is genuinely solid — the independent audit checked out (code review clean, both bug fixes real with tests, 167/167 tests re-run myself, all three criteria actually enforced in the code). But "closed" was declared on the strength of that alone, and discovery landing a dossier in a folder was never the actual goal. I checked the parts nobody had looked at yet:

**The tracking system is empty, not half-built — it's fully built and untested.** `Tracker/Tracker.md`'s kanban has zero cards in every column. `Tracker/Internship - Dashboard.md`'s Dataview queries are correctly wired but show nothing because `Programs/` and `Applying/` contain nothing but one withdrawn worked example from before the automation existed. `Applying/_This Week.md` still says "Nothing active yet" — written 2026-07-16, still true today, with 26 real, live, already-screened dossiers sitting there. Steps 2 through 7 of the Pipeline (commit → outreach → tailor → prep → close) are 100% manual by design and have been run **zero times** against anything the automation produced. That's the actual gap — not a bug, a step that was never taken.

**Contact discovery is real but shallow.** I read `enrich.py` in full: it Firecrawl-searches the company site + engineering blog, scrapes verbatim, pulls public GitHub org members, and guesses `first.last@domain` validated by MX record. It genuinely works as described — and it's never run once (one git commit ever, zero `## Enrichment` sections in any of the 26 dossiers). Its real limit isn't that it's broken, it's that none of those sources reliably find an actual recruiter — a blog byline or random GitHub org member is a lead to research further, not a contact to message. Fine tool, oversold as "done."

**Your OPT instinct was right.** `OPT_EXCLUSION_RE` in `posting_page.py` is a literal-phrase regex built from exactly one real example (Anduril's wording) plus two named signals. It's correctly permissive-by-default, but it's exactly as strict as the handful of phrasings it was written from — real postings phrase citizenship/clearance requirements many ways it's never been tested against. That's a structural consequence of "no LLM in the unattended loop," not a mistake — the fix is a feedback mechanism (below), not a smarter regex written from imagination.

**On sourcing — I didn't take the other session's answer at face value, and it's weaker than it looks.** "Manually audit companies," "check AngelList/Crunchbase," "Firecrawl-scrape career pages weekly" — all real options, but there's a better one it missed entirely: most company career pages run on **Greenhouse, Lever, or Ashby**, and all three expose free, public, unauthenticated JSON APIs with the full job description already in the response — `boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/<token>/jobs`, `api.lever.co/v0/postings/<token>`, `api.ashbyhq.com/posting-api/job-board/<token>`. That closes the actual gap (mid-size/niche companies SimplifyJobs' human curators never PR in) without touching Firecrawl at all for the hourly loop, fits the existing `Listing`/`_matches_*` architecture with zero redesign, and the seed list is nearly free — several dossiers already link to these platforms, so their tokens can be harvested straight out of `url` fields already sitting in the vault.

I wrote all of this up in **`20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Research Loop - Improvement Plan.md`**, ordered by leverage — Priority 1 is deliberately "promote 3-5 real dossiers by hand this week," before any new code, because every claim about how well the Tracker or `enrich.py` work is untested until real data has gone through them. I also restated the Source of Truth note's end goal in plain terms (applications submitted, not dossiers written — the metric that's actually still at zero) and fixed a stale section in the Pipeline workflow note that still described the old, never-built Slack automation design.

What do you want to tackle first — running Priority 1 yourself right now on a few real dossiers, or should I help build the Greenhouse/Lever ingestion module next?

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Layer 5 — on-demand company/contact enrichment for one promoted dossier.

Run manually at Step 2 (Commit) of the vault's Internship Pipeline workflow —
deliberately NOT called from run_pipeline.py and never automatic on dossiers
the discovery loop writes:

    FIRECRAWL_API_KEY=... python enrich.py "<path to dossier .md>"

Public sources only: company site/blog scraped via Firecrawl, GitHub org
public members, blog author bylines, pattern-inferred first.last@domain
checked against the domain's MX records (DNS-over-HTTPS — no new dependency).
Hard line per the plan: no LinkedIn, no CAPTCHA bypass, no cookie injection,
no stealth browsing, no login walls. Anything behind one gets skipped.

No LLM call anywhere — scraped text is trimmed verbatim, never summarized.
"""
import os
import re
import sys
from datetime import date
from urllib.parse import urlparse

import requests
import yaml

FIRECRAWL = "https://api.firecrawl.dev/v1"
TIMEOUT = 30


def read_dossier(text: str) -> dict:
    m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
    if not m:
        raise ValueError("no YAML frontmatter found — is this a dossier file?")
    return yaml.safe_load(m.group(1))


def replace_enrichment(text: str, section: str) -> str:
    """Idempotent: a re-run replaces the previous Enrichment section."""
    base = re.split(r"\n## Enrichment\b", text)[0].rstrip("\n")
    return base + "\n\n" + section


def _fc(path: str, payload: dict, key: str) -> dict:
    r = requests.post(f"{FIRECRAWL}/{path}", json=payload, timeout=TIMEOUT,
                      headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {key}"})
    r.raise_for_status()
    return r.json()


def fc_search(query: str, key: str) -> list:
    return _fc("search", {"query": query, "limit": 5}, key).get("data", [])


def fc_scrape(url: str, key: str) -> str:
    data = _fc("scrape", {"url": url, "formats": ["markdown"]}, key).get("data", {})
    return data.get("markdown", "")


def trim(md: str, limit: int = 800) -> str:
    # ponytail: naive trim keeps nav/link junk at the top of some pages —
    # good enough to orient a human; add a boilerplate stripper if it annoys.
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", md).strip()[:limit]


BYLINE_RE = re.compile(r"\b[Bb]y[: ]+\[?([A-Z][a-z]+ [A-Z][a-z]+(?:-[A-Z][a-z]+)?)\]?")


def extract_bylines(md: str) -> list:
    return sorted(set(BYLINE_RE.findall(md)))


def github_org_members(company: str) -> tuple:
    """Best-match public GitHub org and up to 5 public members. Unauthenticated
    (60 req/hr is plenty for an on-demand tool); uses GITHUB_TOKEN if set."""
    h = {"Accept": "application/vnd.github+json"}
    if os.environ.get("GITHUB_TOKEN"):
        h["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {os.environ['GITHUB_TOKEN']}"
    r = requests.get("https://api.github.com/search/users",
                     params={"q": f"{company} type:org", "per_page": 1},
                     headers=h, timeout=TIMEOUT)
    if r.status_code != 200 or not r.json().get("items"):
        return None, []
    org = r.json()["items"][0]["login"]
    r = requests.get(f"https://api.github.com/orgs/{org}/public_members",
                     params={"per_page": 5}, headers=h, timeout=TIMEOUT)
    members = []
    for m in (r.json() if r.status_code == 200 else []):
        detail = requests.get(m["url"], headers=h, timeout=TIMEOUT).json()
        members.append((detail.get("name") or m["login"], m["html_url"]))
    return org, members


def mx_ok(domain: str) -> bool:
    """MX lookup via DNS-over-HTTPS — validates the domain accepts mail,
    not that the specific inferred address exists."""
    try:
        r = requests.get("https://dns.google/resolve",
                         params={"name": domain, "type": "MX"}, timeout=TIMEOUT)
        return bool(r.json().get("Answer"))
    except requests.RequestException:
        return False


def infer_email(name: str, domain: str):
    parts = re.sub(r"[^a-z \-]", "", name.lower()).replace("-", "").split()
    if len(parts) < 2 or not domain:
        return None
    return f"{parts[0]}.{parts[-1]}@{domain}"


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 2:
        sys.exit(__doc__)
    key = os.environ.get("FIRECRAWL_API_KEY")
    if not key:
        sys.exit("FIRECRAWL_API_KEY is not set — get one at firecrawl.dev and "
                 "export it before running.")
    path = sys.argv[1]
    text = open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    company = read_dossier(text)["company"]
    print(f"Enriching {company} …")

    sources, contacts = [], []
    site_url = about = blog_url = ""
    hits = fc_search(f"{company} official site", key)
    if hits:
        site_url = hits[0]["url"]
        sources.append(site_url)
        about = trim(fc_scrape(site_url, key))
    domain = urlparse(site_url).netloc.removeprefix("www.") if site_url else ""

    for hit in fc_search(f"{company} engineering blog", key):
        if re.search(r"blog|engineering|tech", hit["url"], re.I):
            blog_url = hit["url"]
            sources.append(blog_url)
            for name in extract_bylines(fc_scrape(blog_url, key)):
                contacts.append((name, f"blog byline ({blog_url})"))
            break

    org, members = github_org_members(company)
    if org:
        sources.append(f"https://github.com/orgs/{org}/people")
        contacts += [(n, u) for n, u in members]

    mx = mx_ok(domain) if domain else False
    rows = []
    for name, src in contacts:
        email = infer_email(name, domain)
        rows.append(f"| {name} | {src} | {email or '—'} | "
                    f"{'✓' if email and mx else '—'} |")

    today = date.today().isoformat()
    section = "\n".join(
        [f"## Enrichment ({today})",
         f"- **Site:** {site_url or 'not found'}",
         f"- **About (scraped, verbatim):** {about or '—'}",
         f"- **Engineering blog:** {blog_url or 'not found'}",
         f"- **GitHub org:** {f'https://github.com/{org}' if org else 'not found'}",
         f"- **Email domain MX:** {'valid' if mx else 'no MX record found — inferred emails unvalidated'}",
         "", "### Contacts (public sources only — verify before sending anything)",
         "| Name | Source | Inferred email | MX |", "| --- | --- | --- | --- |"]
        + (rows or ["| — | none found | — | — |"])
        + ["", "### Sources"] + [f"- {s}" for s in sources]) + "\n"

    open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(replace_enrichment(text, section))
    print(f"Wrote Enrichment section: {len(contacts)} contact(s), "
          f"{len(sources)} source(s) → {path}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

"""Discovery-time posting-page fetch: one Firecrawl call per NEW match serves
both dossier content (verbatim extraction, trimmed) and the OPT-eligibility
check. Fail-open by design — a Firecrawl outage writes a thin dossier and
never blocks discovery. No LLM call: Firecrawl returns page markdown, the
extraction below is mechanical line filtering.

OPT semantics (per the Phase 6 decision in the Run note): OPT is work
authorization the F-1 student already holds — NOT H-1B sponsorship. Exclude
only on an explicit negative signal: citizenship/US-person requirement,
security-clearance requirement, or an explicit OPT/CPT-not-accepted
statement. "No visa sponsorship" and "background investigation" do NOT
exclude. Signals are checked PER POSTING, not per company — verified against
real data 2026-07-18: Palantir's US Government and Commercial internships
differ on exactly this axis within the same company.
"""
import re

import requests

FIRECRAWL_SCRAPE_URL = "https://api.firecrawl.dev/v1/scrape"
FETCH_TIMEOUT = 120
CONTENT_LIMIT = 7000

# Built from the actual exclusion language found on live posting pages
# 2026-07-18 (Anduril: "U.S. Person status is required as this position needs
# to access export controlled data") plus the Phase 6 note's two other named
# signals. Deliberately NOT matched: EEO boilerplate ("without regard to ...
# citizenship status"), veteran definitions, and Palantir's conditional
# "willingness to undergo a background investigation".
OPT_EXCLUSION_RE = re.compile(
    r"(u\.?s\.? person (status )?(is )?required"
    r"|must be a u\.?s\.? (citizen|person)"
    r"|u\.?s\.? citizenship (is )?required"
    r"|requires? u\.?s\.? citizenship"
    r"|(active|current) (u\.?s\.? )?(security )?clearance (is )?required"
    r"|must (hold|possess|have) (an? )?(active |current )?(u\.?s\.? )?security clearance"
    r"|(opt|cpt)( candidates?| students?)? (are |is )?not (accepted|eligible|supported))",
    re.I,
)


def opt_exclusion(text: str):
    """The matched exclusion phrase, or None if the posting shows no explicit
    negative signal (permissive default, like every other filter here)."""
    m = OPT_EXCLUSION_RE.search(text)
    return m.group(0) if m else None


def fetch_posting_markdown(url: str, api_key: str, http_post=None) -> str:
    """Page markdown via Firecrawl (JS-rendered — ATS pages are SPAs).
    Raises requests exceptions on failure; callers treat any failure as
    'no data' and fail open."""
    resp = (http_post or requests.post)(
        FIRECRAWL_SCRAPE_URL,
        headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {api_key}"},
        json={"url": url, "formats": ["markdown"], "waitFor": 8000},
        timeout=FETCH_TIMEOUT,
    )
    resp.raise_for_status()
    return resp.json().get("data", {}).get("markdown", "")


_CUT_MARKERS = re.compile(
    r"^(#+\s*)?(submit your application|apply for this job|autofill.*application|create a job alert"
    r"|equal (employment )?opportunity|eeo|voluntary self.identification|privacy (policy|notice)"
    r"|u\.s\. equal employment|by applying.*you (agree|acknowledge))", re.I)
_NOISE = re.compile(
    r"^(\[?!\[|\[back to jobs|\[apply\]|apply\b|select\.\.\.|✱|.*✱\s*$|resume/cv|full name|email\b"
    r"|phone\b|current location|current company|linkedin url|github url|portfolio url|loading$"
    r"|no location found|couldn't auto-read|analyzing resume|success!$|file exceeds|-{3,}$"
    r"|cookie|jobs powered by|©|powered by\s|\[.*\]\(https?://[^)]*\)\s*$)", re.I)


def extract_content(markdown: str, limit: int = CONTENT_LIMIT) -> str:
    """The posting's substantive text: from the first real heading up to the
    application-form/EEO chrome, minus nav/form/boilerplate lines. Verbatim
    lines, never a summary. Blank lines and '---' rules dropped to satisfy
    the vault's format conventions (see validate.check_format_compliance)."""
    out, started = [], False
    for line in markdown.splitlines():
        s = line.strip()
        if _CUT_MARKERS.match(s):
            break
        if not started:
            if s.startswith("#") and len(s) > 4:
                started = True
            else:
                continue
        if not s or _NOISE.match(s):
            continue
        out.append(s)
        if len("\n".join(out)) > limit:
            break
    return "\n".join(out)
```

# Layer 2 filter config — see Research Loop Implementation Plan for rationale.
grad_year: 2028
class_year: junior
eligible_class_tags: [Junior, "3rd year"]  # matched as case-insensitive substrings against target_year entries
accept_unrestricted: true  # postings with no class-year field at all still match
# "Winter 2027" = Dec 2026-Feb 2027 in SimplifyJobs' taxonomy — winters are
# labeled by the LATER year, confirmed 2026-07-18 by term adjacency in live data
# (three real listings co-tag ["Fall 2026", "Winter 2027"]). Postings don't
# publish actual months in the feed, so the human screen of the fetched posting
# content is still what confirms a genuine Dec-Jan window per posting.
terms: ["Summer 2027", "Winter 2027"]
# Real category values observed on SimplifyJobs/Summer2026-Internships (dev/.github/scripts/listings.json),
# not the generic names in the original transcript — confirmed by fetching live data 2026-07-16.
categories: ["Software", "Software Engineering", "AI/ML/Data", "Data Science, AI & Machine Learning"]
# Rejects a SimplifyJobs listing outright if any of these terms are present, even
# alongside an allowed term (multi-term/rotational postings spanning both cycles).
exclude_terms: ["Summer 2026", "Fall 2026", "Spring 2026"]
# Rule built 2026-07-17 from live feed data (1216 distinct location strings), not
# guessed — see location_eligible() in core/filter.py. Permissive: no location
# data or ambiguous strings ('Multiple Locations', 'Virtual', bare 'Remote') still
# match; only affirmatively non-US listings (Canada/UK/'Remote in Germany') drop.
locations_allow: us_remote
# Real live values use the apostrophe form ("Bachelor's" 5450, ("Master's","PhD")
# 592, ("PhD",) 586, empty 4676 — fetched 2026-07-18). Permissive: empty degrees
# data passes; non-empty must intersect this list.
degrees_allow: ["Bachelor's"]

- ## What This System Is

+ ## What This System Is
A GitHub Actions workflow in the public repo `gupta-builds/internship-research-loop` polls internship-listing sources on a schedule, filters them against a fixed profile with zero Claude/Anthropic LLM calls anywhere in the loop, and writes the ones that pass into `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Dossiers/` in this vault — deterministic field-matching and mechanical checks only, by design, so a 24/7 background process never spends Claude tokens.
## The End Goal, Stated Plainly
This exists so that a real Summer 2027 or Dec 2026–Jan 2027 SWE/AI/data internship, open in the US and eligible for OPT, reaches this vault within an hour of going live *anywhere it's discoverable*, already carrying enough real content that deciding to pursue it takes 60 seconds, not a webpage visit. Discovery landing a dossier in `List/Dossiers/` is not the finish line — **the finish line is a submitted application**, and the loop only earns its cost once dossiers are actually being promoted through [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]] into `Programs/` + `Applying/` and turning into real outreach and real submissions. The success metric for this whole project is applications submitted per week and coverage against real postings that exist, not dossiers written or tests passing — both of the latter can be perfect while the actual goal (offers) sees zero progress, which is exactly the risk this note exists to name plainly. [[20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Research Loop - Improvement Plan]] is the honest, evidence-based account of where that finish line currently stands and what closes the gap.

# Research Loop — Improvement Plan
==The honest account of what's actually broken or missing, verified directly against the live repo and the live vault on 2026-07-19, not carried from any prior session's summary. The independent audit that day confirmed the discovery pipeline's code is genuinely solid — but the loop was declared "closed" without a single dossier ever being promoted, without any source beyond two GitHub-curated lists, and with contact discovery and OPT detection real but shallow. This note orders the real gaps by leverage and gives each one a concrete next step, not just a diagnosis.== [[20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Research Loop - Source of Truth]] states what the system was supposed to become; this note states what's still standing between here and there.
## The Real Verdict: Discovery Works, Nothing Downstream Does
26 real, live, currently-open dossiers sit in `10_Areas/Career/Internships/List/Dossiers/` right now — checked against Summer 2027/Winter 2027 timing, US location, and OPT eligibility, most carrying real posting content. Precisely **zero** of them have been promoted through [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]]. `Tracker/Tracker.md`'s kanban has an empty card in every column. `Tracker/Internship - Dashboard.md`'s Pipeline tables are correctly built but show nothing, because `Programs/` and `Applying/` hold nothing but one withdrawn 2026-07-16 worked example. `Applying/_This Week.md` still reads "Nothing active yet... First real entry lands once the research loop or a manual find promotes something Junior-eligible" — written three days and 26 real matches ago, still true today. The bottleneck was never discovery. It's Step 2 ("Commit") onward in the Pipeline — 100% manual by design, and exercised zero times against real automation output. Calling this "closed" while that's true is why it feels like a waste of time: the thing that was supposed to change — applications going out — hasn't moved at all.
## Priority 1 — Prove the promotion step works, on real data, this week
Before writing one more line of automation: pick 3-5 of the 26 real dossiers (a mix worth stress-testing different shapes — e.g. Rippling, SIG, Optiver, Western Digital) and run Steps 2 through 5 of the Pipeline by hand, once, for real. Create the Programs + Applying notes, draft one real outreach message from `Contacts/Mimic.md`, tailor one resume cut. This is the single highest-leverage thing to do next — every claim about how well the Dashboard, the Kanban, or `enrich.py` work is untested speculation until real data has passed through them. If Step 2 turns out to be slow or annoying in practice, that's the actual next automation target, found by using the system instead of guessing what it needs.
## Priority 2 — Stop relying on two curated lists for discovery
### What's actually true about SimplifyJobs and JGCL
SimplifyJobs is large (14,900+ live entries) and well-maintained, but every entry in it exists because a human noticed a posting and opened a PR — it's downstream of someone else's curation, with the lag and blind spots that implies (mid-size and niche companies that don't get PR'd in are invisible to this pipeline no matter how well the filter works). JGCL (112 entries, last real maintenance mid-2025) is small and thin enough that it barely moves the needle. Both were the right *starting* sources — machine-readable, no login wall, cheap — but "the two sources that happened to be easy to find first" is not the same as "comprehensive coverage," and nothing since Phase 1 has revisited that.
### The better answer: poll ATS platforms directly, not career pages via Firecrawl
Most companies' application pages are hosted on a small number of ATS platforms that expose **public, unauthenticated, structured JSON APIs** — not scraped HTML, not something Firecrawl needs to touch at all:
- **Greenhouse:** `https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/<token>/jobs?content=true` — full JD text included in the response.
- **Lever:** `https://api.lever.co/v0/postings/<token>?mode=json` — same.
- **Ashby:** `https://api.ashbyhq.com/posting-api/job-board/<token>` — same.
These are exactly the kind of source this pipeline already knows how to consume — structured JSON, no auth, matches the existing `Listing` dataclass / per-source `_matches_*` pattern in `core/filter.py` with zero architectural change, just new modules in `ingestion/`. This closes the real gap the two curated lists have (mid-size/niche companies that never get PR'd into SimplifyJobs) without adding a single Firecrawl call to the hourly discovery loop, and it's lower-latency than either curated list, since it's not waiting on a human to notice and PR the listing.
### The seed list is nearly free to build
Every dossier already written carries the posting's real `url` — several are already Greenhouse or Lever links (The Trade Desk's dossier already used the Greenhouse board API as a fallback for its content fetch, per the Phase 6 closing pass). A one-time pass over every `url` field across all 26 (and every future) dossier extracts the ATS platform + company token for free — no manual company list needed to start. Firecrawl's actual job here is a small, low-frequency **company-discovery** search (e.g. weekly, a handful of calls) to find companies not yet represented in any dossier, and as the fallback content-fetch path for the companies that run something non-standard (Workday, custom sites) — exactly its current role, just not stretched into "the primary hourly discovery mechanism," which it was never well-suited for.
### Sequencing this
1. Write a small script that greps every existing dossier's `url` for a Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby pattern and lists the tokens found — this alone tells you how many of the 26 are already on a pollable ATS, with zero new code risk.
2. Add `ingestion/greenhouse.py` / `ingestion/lever.py` following the exact shape of `ingestion/sources.py`'s existing fetchers, feeding the same filter/dedup/write-gate pipeline untouched.
3. Seed the token list from step 1, expand it manually with 10-20 known target companies, and only then consider a Firecrawl-based discovery-search step for growing the list further.
## Priority 3 — Contact discovery and OPT detection are real, not fake, but shallower than "closed" implied
### `enrich.py` (Layer 5)
It genuinely does what it claims — Firecrawl-searches the company's official site and engineering blog, scrapes verbatim, pulls public GitHub org members, pattern-infers `first.last@domain` and validates the domain's MX record — and it has never run once against real data (confirmed: one git commit ever, zero `## Enrichment` sections across all 26 real dossiers). Its real limitation isn't that it's broken, it's that none of its contact sources reliably surface an actual recruiter or hiring manager — a blog byline or a random public GitHub org member is a research starting point, not a warm contact. That's a reasonable tool for "who do I even look up," not "who do I message" — worth using once (Priority 1) with that expectation set correctly, not oversold as solved.
### OPT exclusion detection
`OPT_EXCLUSION_RE` in `ingestion/posting_page.py` is a literal-phrase regex built from exactly one real example (Anduril's "U.S. Person status is required") plus the two other named signals from the Phase 6 decision. This is real, tested, and correctly permissive-by-default — but it is also exactly as strict as the handful of phrasings it was built from, and real job postings phrase citizenship/clearance requirements many different ways it has never been tested against. This is the accurate version of "the OPT rule is strict and not implemented correctly": it's not implemented *wrong*, it's implemented *narrow*, because it's regex matching standing in for judgment a design choice (no LLM in the unattended loop) deliberately keeps out of the hourly run. The fix isn't a smarter regex written from imagination — see Priority 4.
## Priority 4 — Give the loop a real feedback mechanism, not just a test suite
The existing "self-improving" design (a false-positive filter match gets caught, then becomes a permanent regression test) only works if a human actually notices a mismatch and reports it — nothing currently invites that. Concrete, minimal mechanism, consistent with every other design choice in this project (promotion-triggered, never automatic, never unattended):
- Add a `rejection_reason` field a human fills in on a dossier or Applying note when a match turns out wrong during screening (bad OPT call, wrong location, irrelevant category, whatever).
- A periodic review pass (weekly to start, whenever there's enough real data to be worth a session) reads every `rejection_reason` set since the last pass, looks for real patterns, and turns them into either a `profile.yaml` change or an `OPT_EXCLUSION_RE` addition — each landing as a permanent regression test, exactly like every fix so far in this project's history.
- This is the actual mechanism that makes "learns from its mistakes" true instead of aspirational — it doesn't exist yet because nothing has been promoted or screened yet (Priority 1 again) to generate a single real rejection to learn from.
## What "100x" Actually Means Here
Stop measuring this system by dossiers written or tests passing — both can be perfect while the real goal, applications submitted, sits at zero, which is exactly what happened. The metrics that matter: applications submitted per week, and coverage (spot-checked periodically by manually auditing 15-20 target companies' real career pages against what the pipeline actually caught) — not raw source count or dossier volume.
## Sequencing, In Order
1. **This week:** Priority 1 — promote 3-5 real dossiers by hand, generate real friction data.
2. **Next:** Priority 2 step 1 — the free ATS-token audit over existing dossier URLs, then the Greenhouse/Lever ingestion modules.
3. **Then:** Priority 3 — use `enrich.py` once for real (part of Priority 1's promotion pass) and decide, from real output, whether it's worth investing further or leaving as a "starting point" tool.
4. **Ongoing, once there's real data:** Priority 4 — wire the `rejection_reason` field and start the periodic review pass.
## What Not To Touch Yet
The discovery pipeline itself — ingestion, the profile filter, dedup, the write gate, the three hard criteria, the recheck/mass-deletion-brake hardening — is independently verified working as of the 2026-07-19 audit. None of that needs rebuilding; the entire gap identified here is downstream of discovery, not inside it.

```

This is explicitly not built yet. The pipeline above works by hand first; automation gets wired into Step 1 once the manual version is proven.

## [2026-07-19] audit | Internship loop — the real gap is promotion, not discovery
- The independent audit (run against the prompt from this session's earlier entry) came back: code review clean, both claimed bug fixes real with regression tests, 167/167 tests independently re-run and confirmed, all three hard criteria genuinely enforced in `profile.yaml`/`filter.py`, zero LinkedIn/CAPTCHA/LLM violations found anywhere in the source tree. One real new finding: the one thin (948-byte) Palantir dossier is explained — its stored `url` is a Lever `/apply` form endpoint, not the JD page, so Firecrawl fetched successfully but there was almost nothing to extract; distinct from the fail-open path, not yet handled as its own case. Also found: `state/opt_cache.json`'s 6 live entries have zero overlap with `Companies giving OPT & CPT.md`'s ~20 manually-audited entries — the vault note was never updated once the machine cache started actually writing, and nothing in the note flags that the two lists cover disjoint time periods.
- User pushback, and it was correct: closing the loop on code correctness said nothing about whether the system is actually useful yet. Independently verified by reading `Tracker/Tracker.md` (empty kanban, every column), `Tracker/Internship - Dashboard.md` (correctly built, empty by construction), and `Applying/_This Week.md` (still "Nothing active yet," written 2026-07-16, still true 2026-07-19 with 26 real dossiers sitting unpromoted) — zero of the 26 real, live, currently-open dossiers have ever been promoted through [[30_Order/Workflows/Internship Pipeline]]. The discovery half of this project is solid; the half that actually produces applications has a 0% completion rate against real automation output. - Also found and fixed a real coverage gap in the sourcing strategy: relying on SimplifyJobs + JGCL alone means every match is downstream of a human curator noticing and PR'ing a listing. Proposed a structurally better fix — Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby ATS platforms expose free, public, unauthenticated JSON job-board APIs with full JD content included, pollable on the same hourly cadence with zero new Firecrawl cost, and the seed list is nearly free to bootstrap since several existing dossier `url` fields already point at these platforms.
